L^{q}(\mathscr{O})\hookrightarrow L^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathscr{O})\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\mathscr{O})\hookrightarrow L^{\sigma}(\mathscr{O})\hookrightarrow L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathscr{O}).

Proof.

Observe that the definition of
\sigma
gives that:

\sigma\geq\dfrac{5q}{2q+5}.

A simple calculation gives:

\dfrac{5q}{2q+5}\geq\dfrac{q}{q-1}\quad\textup{ if and only if }q\geq\dfrac{10}{3}.

Again from the definition of
\sigma
, we obtain that:

\sigma\leq\dfrac{28q}{9q+28}.

Another simple calculation gives:

\dfrac{28q}{9q+28}\leq 2\quad\textup{ if and only if }q\leq\dfrac{28}{5}.

In conclusion, we have shown that
q^{\prime}\leq\sigma\leq 2
. The remaining inequalities for
\sigma^{\prime}
immediately follow from the fact that the mapping
\sigma>1\mapsto\frac{\sigma}{\sigma-1}
is decreasing. The claimed chain of embeddings follows by Hölder’s inequality.
∎

We establish some preliminary results of independent interest for the exponential mapping defined over Bochner spaces, that will be used to study the function
A
introduced in (
2.10
).

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
m\geq 1
be an integer, let
\mathscr{O}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
be open and bounded, and let
1\leq q\leq\infty
. For each function
f\in L^{1}(\mathscr{O})
such that
f(x)\leq 0
for a.a.
x\in\mathscr{O}
, we define the
exponential at

f
by:

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{expf}{{{}}\exp(f)}(x):=e^{f(x)},\quad\textup{ for a.a. }x\in\mathscr{O}.

Consider a sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
of non-positive real valued functions
f_{n}:\mathscr{O}\to\mathbb{R}^{-}\cup\{0\}
such that
f_{n}\to f
in
L^{q}(\mathscr{O})
as
n\to\infty
.
Then, it results:

\exp(f_{n})\to\exp(f),\quad\textup{ in }L^{q}(\mathscr{O})\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

Proof.

Let us consider the case where
1\leq q<\infty
. The fact that
f_{n}\leq 0
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
for all integers
n\geq 1
implies that:

0\leq\exp(f_{n})\leq 1,\quad\textup{ a.e. in }\mathscr{O}\textup{ for all integers }n\geq 1.

An application of the Mean Value Theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 9.3-1 in
[
21
]
) gives

(4.5)

\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|\exp(f_{n})-\exp(f)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}\leq\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|f_{n}-f|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q},

and the right-hand side of the latter converges to zero as
n\to\infty
in light of the assumed convergence.

For the case where
q=\infty
, it suffices to observe that an application of the Mean Value Theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 9.3-1 in
[
21
]
) gives

\|\exp(f_{n})-\exp(f)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O})}\leq\|f_{n}-f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O})}\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
in light of the assumed convergence. The proof is complete.
∎

For the purpose of studying the function
\mu
in (
4.7
), we establish the following results of general interest.
The next result complements Lemma
4.13
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
I
be a bounded open interval in
\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathscr{O}
be a bounded open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
, for some integer
m\geq 1
. Assume that
1\leq q\leq\infty
. Let
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
such that:

(a)

For each
t\in\overline{I}
it results
f_{n}(t)\leq 0
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
,

(b)

The sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
to a function
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
.

Then, we obtain that
\{\exp(f_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
to
\exp(f)\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
.

Proof.

Let us consider the case where
1\leq q<\infty
; the case
q=\infty
follows by the same argument with
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O})}
in place of
\|\cdot\|_{L^{q}(\mathscr{O})}
. The proof is divided into four parts, numbered
(i)
–
(iv)
.

(i)

Non-positiveness of the limit.
We first show that
f(t)\leq 0
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
for each
t\in\overline{I}
. By Lemma
4.1
(a)
, the operator
-\{\cdot\}^{-}
is Lipschitz continuous on
L^{q}(\mathscr{O})
. Since
f_{n}(t)\leq 0
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
for each
t\in\overline{I}
by assumption
(a)
, we have
f_{n}(t)=-\{f_{n}(t)\}^{-}
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
for each
t\in\overline{I}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\max_{t\in\overline{I}}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|f_{n}(t)-(-\{f(t)\}^{-})|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}=\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|(-\{f_{n}(t)\}^{-})-(-\{f(t)\}^{-})|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|f_{n}(t)-f(t)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
. Hence, for each
t\in\overline{I}
, the uniqueness of the limit in
L^{q}(\mathscr{O})
gives
f(t)=-\{f(t)\}^{-}=\min\{f(t),0\}\leq 0
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
.

(ii)

A pointwise estimate.
Since
f_{n}(t),f(t)\leq 0
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
for all
t\in\overline{I}
, the exponential function is evaluated on non-positive arguments. Applying the Mean Value Theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 9.3-1 in
[
21
]
) to
\xi\mapsto\exp(\xi)
on the interval between
f_{n}(t)(x)
and
f(t)(x)
yields, for all
t\in\overline{I}
and for a.a.
x\in\mathscr{O}

|\exp(f_{n}(t)(x))-\exp(f(t)(x))|=e^{\xi_{t,x}}\,|f_{n}(t)(x)-f(t)(x)|\leq|f_{n}(t)(x)-f(t)(x)|,

where
\xi_{t,x}\leq 0
and therefore
e^{\xi_{t,x}}\leq 1
.

Applying the same argument as in the estimate above gives, for any
s,t\in\overline{I}

\left\|\exp(f_{n}(t))-\exp(f_{n}(s))\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathscr{O})}\leq\|f_{n}(t)-f_{n}(s)\|_{L^{q}(\mathscr{O})}\to 0,

as
s\to t
, thus showing that
\{\exp(f_{n})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
. The same argument applies to
\exp(f)
.

(iii)

From pointwise to
L^{q}(\mathscr{O})
norm.
Using the monotonicity of
\xi\mapsto\xi^{q}
on
[0,\infty)
, raising the pointwise estimate to the power
q
gives:

|\exp(f_{n}(t)(x))-\exp(f(t)(x))|^{q}\leq|f_{n}(t)(x)-f(t)(x)|^{q},\quad\textup{ for all }t\in\overline{I}\textup{ and for a.a. }x\in\mathscr{O}.

Integrating over
\mathscr{O}
and using the monotonicity of
\xi\mapsto\xi^{1/q}
gives:

\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|\exp(f_{n}(t))-\exp(f(t))|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}\leq\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|f_{n}(t)-f(t)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}.

(iv)

Convergence in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
.
Taking the maximum over
t\in\overline{I}
and using assumption
(b)
gives

\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|\exp(f_{n}(t))-\exp(f(t))|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}\leq\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}}|f_{n}(t)-f(t)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/q}\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
. Finally, the fact that
\exp(f)\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}))
follows from the boundedness of
\mathscr{O}
and the estimate
0\leq\exp(f(t))\leq 1
a.e. in
\mathscr{O}
for all
t\in\overline{I}
, which guarantees
\exp(f(t))\in L^{q}(\mathscr{O})
for each
t\in\overline{I}
, together with the same Lipschitz estimate applied to
f
itself to establish continuity at
t\in\overline{I}
.

The proof for
q=\infty
is analogous, replacing the
L^{q}(\mathscr{O})
norm with the essential supremum.
∎

The next results establishes useful properties for averages in Bochner spaces.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
I
be a bounded open interval in
\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathscr{O}_{1}
be a bounded open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and let
\mathscr{O}_{2}
be an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
, for some integer
m_{1},m_{2}\geq 1
. Assume that
1\leq q\leq\infty
.
Let
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence in
L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
such that
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
.
Then, it results that the sequence

\left\{\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

converges in
L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{2}))
.

Proof.

Let us consider the case where
q
is finite. We first verify that if
f\in L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
then
\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f\,\mathrm{d}x\in L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{2}))
. To this aim, we observe that an application of Hölder’s inequality gives

\int_{I}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\left|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f\,\mathrm{d}x\right|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|^{q-1}\int_{I}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}|f|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t,

and the conclusion follows directly from the Fubini–Tonelli theorem (cf., e.g.,
[
21
]
).

To complete the proof, consider a sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
that converges to a function
f
in
L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
, and observe that

\displaystyle\int_{I}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\left|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f\,\mathrm{d}x\right|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|^{q-1}\int_{I}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}|f_{n}-f|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=|\mathscr{O}_{1}|^{q-1}\int_{I}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}}|f_{n}-f|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t,

as
n\to\infty
, where the estimates holds once again thanks to Hölder’s inequality and Theorem 8.28 in
[
52
]
.

If
q=\infty
, for a.a.
t\in I
and for a.a.
z\in\mathscr{O}_{2}
, another application of the Hölder inequality and Theorem 8.28 in
[
52
]
give:

\left|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}(f_{n}(t)(x,z)-f(t)(x,z))\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|\|f_{n}(t)-f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2})}.

Passing to the essential supremum over
I
in the estimate above gives:

\sup_{t\in I}\left\|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}(f_{n}(t)(x,z)-f(t)(x,z))\,\mathrm{d}x\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O}_{2})}\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|\sup_{t\in I}\|f_{n}(t)-f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2})}.

The assumed convergence for the sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
in turn gives that the right-hand side of the estimate above tends to zero as
n\to\infty
, completing the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
I
be a bounded open interval in
\mathbb{R}
, let
\mathscr{O}_{1}
be a bounded open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and let
\mathscr{O}_{2}
be an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
, for some integer
m_{1},m_{2}\geq 1
. Assume that
1\leq q\leq\infty
. Let
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
such that
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
. Then, it results that the sequence

\left\{\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{2}))
.

Proof.

Let us first consider the case where
q
is finite. We first verify that if
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
then
\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{2}))
. To this aim, we observe that for each
s,t\in\overline{I}
, it results that an application of the Hölder inequality gives

\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\left|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f(s)\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|^{q-1}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}|f(s)-f(t)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\to 0,

when
s\to t
. To complete the proof, consider a Cauchy sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
that converges to
f
in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2}))
, and evaluate

\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left\|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\right\|_{L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{2})}\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|^{1-\frac{1}{q}}\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left(\int_{\mathscr{O}_{2}}\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}|f_{n}(t)-f(t)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\right)^{1/q}\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
, where the estimates holds once again thanks to Hölder’s inequality.

If
q=\infty
, for all
t\in\overline{I}
and for a.a.
z\in\mathscr{O}_{2}
, another application of the Hölder inequality and Theorem 8.28 in
[
52
]
give:

\left|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}(f_{n}(t)(x,z)-f(t)(x,z))\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|\|f_{n}(t)-f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2})}.

taking the maximum over
\overline{I}
in the estimate above gives:

\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\left\|\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}(f_{n}(t)(x,z)-f(t)(x,z))\,\mathrm{d}x\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O}_{2})}\leq|\mathscr{O}_{1}|\max_{t\in\overline{I}}\|f_{n}(t)-f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathscr{O}_{1}\times\mathscr{O}_{2})}.

The assumed convergence for the sequence
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
in turn gives that the right-hand side of the estimate above tends to zero as
n\to\infty
, completing the proof.
∎

Remark 4.17
.

Following a strategy similar to the one presented for establishing Lemma
4.15
, one can show that if
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
L^{q}(I;L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}))
then the sequence
\left\{\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
L^{q}(I)
.

Likewise, if
\{f_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};L^{q}(\mathscr{O}_{1}))
then the sequence
\left\{\int_{\mathscr{O}_{1}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
converges in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I})
.

\blacksquare

Let us now recall a useful result to analyse the spatial gradient of functions defined over Sobolev–Bochner spaces.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
N\geq 1
be an integer, let
\mathscr{O}
be an open set in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
, let
0<T\leq\infty
and let
1<p<\infty
.
Let
\vec{v}\in L^{p}(0,T;W^{1,p}(\mathscr{O}))
. For each integer
1\leq i\leq N
, it is possible to define the mapping
\widetilde{\partial_{i}\vec{v}}:(0,T)\to L^{p}(\mathscr{O})
, which is given by
\widetilde{\partial_{i}\vec{v}}(t):=\partial_{i}(\vec{v}(t))
, for a.a.
t\in(0,T)
.

In particular, if
\{\vec{w}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a sequence such that
\vec{w}_{n}\rightharpoonup\vec{w}
in
L^{p}(0,T;W^{1,p}(\mathscr{O}))
as
n\to\infty
, it results:

\widetilde{\partial_{i}\vec{w}_{n}}\rightharpoonup\widetilde{\partial_{i}\vec{w}},\quad\textup{ in }L^{p}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathscr{O}))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

Proof.

To begin with, let us observe that for each
\vec{v}\in L^{p}(0,T;W^{1,p}(\mathscr{O}))
it results:

(4.6)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathscr{O}}\left|\widetilde{\partial_{i}\vec{v}}(t)\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathscr{O}}\left|\partial_{i}(\vec{v}(t))\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\|\vec{v}\|_{L^{p}(0,T;W^{1,p}(\mathscr{O}))}^{p}.

Let
\{\vec{w}_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence such that
\vec{w}_{n}\rightharpoonup\vec{w}
in
L^{p}(0,T;W^{1,p}(\mathscr{O}))
as
n\to\infty
. In light of (
4.6
), it thus results that the sequence
\{\widetilde{\partial_{i}\vec{w}_{n}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{p}(0,T;L^{p}(\mathscr{O}))
for each
1\leq i\leq N
independently of
n
.

Let
\vec{\varphi}\in\mathcal{D}(0,T;\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{O}))
be given, and recall that
L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathscr{O}))\cong L^{p^{\prime}}((0,T)\times\mathscr{O})
(Theorem 8.28 in
[
52
]
). Let
\phi\in L^{p^{\prime}}((0,T)\times\mathscr{O})
be such that
\phi(t,x)=\vec{\varphi}(t)(x)
. An application of the definition of weak derivative in Bochner spaces gives

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathscr{O}}\partial_{i}(\vec{w}_{n}(t))\vec{\varphi}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathscr{O}}\vec{w}_{n}(t)\partial_{i}\phi(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

and we observe that the right-hand side verifies the convergence

-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathscr{O}}\vec{w}_{n}(t)\partial_{i}\phi(t,\cdot)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\to-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathscr{O}}\vec{w}(t)\partial_{i}(\vec{\varphi}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

as
n\to\infty
. The proof is complete upon observing that
\mathcal{D}(0,T;\mathcal{D}(\mathscr{O}))
is dense in
L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathscr{O}))
.
∎

The next preliminary result we recall, is a generalised integration-by-parts formula, whose proof hinges on the Lebesgue theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 2.11-3 of
[
20
]
).

Lemma 4.19

(Lemma 1.2 of
[
65
]
)
.

Let
\Omega
be a Lipschitz domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and let
T>0
.
Let
\alpha
and
p
be two real numbers greater than
1
, and let
v
be a function such that

\displaystyle v\in L^{p}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\cap\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|v|^{\alpha-2}v)\in L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)).

Then, the following formula holds

\int_{0}^{T}\left\langle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|v|^{\alpha-2}v),v\right\rangle\,\mathrm{d}t=\dfrac{\|v(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}-\dfrac{\|v(0)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}},

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the duality between
W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
and
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
.
∎

We now state the well-known Dubinskii’s lemma (cf., e.g., Theorem 1 of
[
29
]
, Theorem 2.1 of
[
5
]
, Theorem 3 in
[
18
]
or Theorem 1 in
[
19
]
).

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
be Banach spaces such that
A_{0}\hookrightarrow A_{1}
. Let
S\subset A_{0}
be such that
\lambda S\subset S
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.

Assume that the set
S
is endowed with the definite homogeneous gauge
M:S\to\mathbb{R}
, that satisfies the following properties:

•

M(v)\geq 0
, for all
v\in S
,

•

M(\lambda v)=|\lambda|M(v)
, for all
v\in S
and all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.

Assume that the set
\mathscr{M}:=\{v\in S;M(v)\leq 1\}
is relatively compact in
A_{0}
. Consider the semi-normed set

Y:=\left\{u\in L^{1}_{\textup{loc}}(0,T;A_{1});\int_{0}^{T}[M(u(t))]^{q_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}t<\infty\textup{ and }\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}u}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(t)\right\|_{A_{1}}^{q_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}t<\infty\right\},

with
1\leq q_{0}\leq\infty
and
1\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
, and the pairs
(q_{0},q_{1})=(1,\infty)
and
(q_{0},q_{1})=(\infty,1)
cannot be attained.
Then, it results that:

(a)

Y
is relatively compact in
L^{q_{0}}(0,T;A_{0})
when
1\leq q_{0}<\infty
;

(b)

Y
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];A_{0})
when
q_{0}=\infty
.

∎

Finally, we establish a sharp generalised Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality for the space
V
, giving an explicit construction for the constant.

Lemma 4.21
.

The following generalised Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality holds:

\|\Xi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}\leq\sqrt{2}L\|\nabla\Xi\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))},\quad\textup{ for all }\Xi\in V.

Proof.

We reflect the the Lipschitz domain
\Omega\times(0,L)
across the plane
\{z=0\}
by even extension. As a result, with each
\Xi\in V
, we can associate the function
\hat{\Xi}\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega\times(-L,L))
defined by:

\hat{\Xi}(x,z):=\begin{cases}\Xi(x,z)&,\textup{ if }(x,z)\in\Omega\times(0,L),\\
\Xi(x,-z)&,\textup{ if }(x,z)\in\Omega\times(-L,0).\end{cases}

Recall that, in the special case where
\overline{\Omega}
can be regarded as a surface in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with zero torsion, the best constant for Theorem 9.7 in
[
11
]
is given by
\sqrt{2}
. Therefore, an application of the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.3-3
(a)
in
[
21
]
) gives

\sqrt{2}\|\Xi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}=\|\hat{\Xi}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(-L,L))}\leq\sqrt{2}L\|\nabla\hat{\Xi}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(-L,L))}=2L\|\nabla\Xi\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))},

from which the completion of the proof immediately follows.
∎

The coupling term
\mu
defined in (
2.20
) takes the following form in the context of the framework we are adopting,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mu(\tilde{\Theta}):=2\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{1}\left(\max\left\{A_{0}\exp\left(-\dfrac{\beta\sqrt{L}}{|\Omega|^{1/2}}\int_{\Omega}\left\{\tilde{\Theta}(t,x,s^{\prime}L)-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\right\}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\right),A_{\textup{min}}\right\}^{+}\right)(1-s^{\prime})^{p}\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

and the bounds (
2.21
) continue to hold. The simplification proposed in (
4.7
) will be critical to establish the validity of the energy estimates in Theorem
5.1
.

We now state the penalised problem, which is suggested by the formal model (
3.25
)–(
3.31
). Observe that the validity of the initial conditions makes sense thanks to Lemma
4.5
. In all what follows, we denote by
ℓ
a
positive penalty parameter
meant to approach zero.

Problem
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}
.

Find function
u

ℓ

and

~

Θ

ℓ

satisfying

\displaystyle u_{\ell}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}\right)

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))),

satisfying the variational equations

(4.8)

\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell})v\,\mathrm{d}x+\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\ell}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x-\dfrac{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell}\}^{-}v\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t,x)v\,\mathrm{d}x,

in the sense of distributions on
(0,T)
for all
v\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
, the variational equations

(4.9)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}+U_{1}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\partial x_{1}}+U_{2}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\partial x_{2}}+v_{z}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\partial z}\right)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\cdot\nabla\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\}^{-}\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}A(\cdot,\cdot,\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x,

in the sense of distributions on
(0,T)
for all
\Xi\in V
, as well as the following initial conditions

\displaystyle u_{\ell}(0)=u_{0}

\displaystyle,\quad\textup{ a.e. in }\Omega,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(0)=\tilde{\Theta}_{0}

\displaystyle,\quad\textup{ a.e. in }\Omega\times(0,L),

for some prescribed non-zero
u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}\cap\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{\Omega})
and
\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\in K_{\textup{temp}}
.

\blacksquare

We first establish the existence of solutions for Problem

P
ℓ

by means of a semi-discrete scheme. Define the operator
-\Delta_{b}:V\to V^{\prime}
by:

\langle-\Delta_{b}\Xi,\Theta\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}:=\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\nabla\Xi\cdot\nabla\Theta\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{q_{\textup{geo}^{\perp}}}{\kappa}\Theta\,\mathrm{d}x,

for all
\Xi,\Theta\in V
. To establish the monotonicity of
-\Delta_{b}
, observe that for each
\Xi,\Theta\in V

\langle-\Delta_{b}\Xi-(-\Delta_{b}\Theta),\Xi-\Theta\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}=\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(\Xi-\Theta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\geq\dfrac{\|\Xi-\Theta\|_{V}^{2}}{1+2L^{2}},

where the last estimate is due to Lemma
4.21
. The same strategy can be employed to establish the coerciveness of the operator
-\Delta_{b}
.

To begin with, we divide the interval
[0,T]
into
N
sub-intervals of length
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{k}{{{}}k}:=T/N
. This gives rise to
N+1
equally-spaced nodes in the interval
[0,T]
.
For each
0\leq n\leq N-1
, consider the following semi-discretisation of Problem

P
ℓ

.

Problem
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}^{n+1}
.

Given
u_{\ell,k}^{n}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
and
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\in V
, find a function
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{un+1}{{{}}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
and a function
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{Tn+1}{{{}}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}}\in V
that satisfy the following variational equations:

(4.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\dfrac{1}{k}\left\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\}-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})\nabla\cdot\left(|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\right)-\dfrac{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}{\ell}\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\\
&=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,\quad\textup{ in }W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),\end{aligned}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\rho c\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}+\rho c(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}+\kappa(-\Delta_{b})\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\dfrac{\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}}{\ell}+\dfrac{\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}}{\ell}\\
&=2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}A(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p},\quad\textup{ in }V^{\prime},\end{aligned}

where
u_{\ell,k}^{0}:=u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}
and
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{0}:=\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\in K_{\textup{temp}}
are the prescribed element appearing in the initial conditions for Problem

P
ℓ

.

\blacksquare

The next lemma establishes a regularity result for the right-hand side of (
4.11
).

Lemma 4.22
.

For each integer
n=0,\dots,N-1
, define:

h_{\ell,k}^{n}:=2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}A(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}.

Then, it results that
h_{\ell,k}^{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,L))
and,
a fortiori
, in
V^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since
u_{\ell,k}^{n}\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
,
\Omega
is a Lipschitz domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
p>2
, the continuity of the positive part (Lemma
4.1
and Remark
4.2
) and the continuity of the mappings
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\cup\{0\}\mapsto t^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\cup\{0\}\mapsto t^{p}
give:

\left(\left\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L]).

The definition of
A
in (
2.10
) in turn gives that
A(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,L))
, thus verifying that
h_{\ell,k}^{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,L))
. Thanks to the Sobolev embedding theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.4-1 in
[
21
]
) and to the density of
V
in
L^{6}(\Omega\times(0,L))
, we obtain that
L^{6/5}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow V^{\prime}
. The fact that
h_{\ell,k}^{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega\times(0,L))
is sufficient to deduce that
h_{\ell,k}^{n}\in V^{\prime}
.
∎

The following existence-and-uniqueness result can be established.

Theorem 4.23
.

Let
T>0
,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
p
be as in Section
4
and let
\alpha
be as in (
4.1
). Let
\ell>0
be given, let
N\geq 1
be an integer, and define
k:=T/N
. Assume that
(H\ref{H1})
–
(H\ref{H4})
hold and that the positive constant
L
satisfies the following upper bound:

(4.12)

L\leq\dfrac{\sqrt{2}\kappa}{4\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}}.

Then, for each integer
0\leq n\leq N-1
, it results that Problem

P
ℓ

+
n
1

admits a unique solution
(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1},\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\times V
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two parts, numbered
(i)
and
(ii)
.

(i)

The variational equations (
4.11
) admit a unique solution
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\in V
.

Consider the operator
\tilde{T}_{\ell}:V\to V^{\prime}
defined by:

\tilde{T}_{\ell}\Xi:=\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\Xi+\rho c(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\Xi+\kappa(-\Delta_{b})\Xi-\dfrac{\{\Xi\}^{-}}{\ell}+\dfrac{\{\Xi-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}}{\ell}.

The operator
\tilde{T}_{\ell}
is hemi-continuous, as each of its terms is hemi-continuous; note in particular that the advection term is linear, the penalty terms are Lipschitz continuous by Lemma
4.1
and Remark
4.2
.

Since the advection term is not, in general, monotone, we discuss the strict monotonicity of the operator
\tilde{T}_{\ell}
separately. Given
\Theta,\Xi\in V
, an application of Lemma
4.1
, Remark
4.2
, Lemma
4.21
and (
4.12
) give:

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{T}_{\ell}\Theta-\tilde{T}_{\ell}\Xi,\Theta-\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}=\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\Theta-\Xi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}((\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla(\Theta-\Xi))(\Theta-\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\nabla(\Theta-\Xi)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\left((-\{\Theta\}^{-})-(-\{\Xi\}^{-})\right)(\Theta-\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\left(\{\Theta-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}-\{\Xi-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\right)(\Theta-\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\|\Theta-\Xi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\left(\kappa-\sqrt{2}\rho cL\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega})}\right)\|\nabla(\Theta-\Xi)\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{\dfrac{\rho c}{k},\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\right\}\|\Theta-\Xi\|_{V}^{2}.

Let us show that the operator
\tilde{T}_{\ell}
is coercive. An application of Young’s inequality
[
73
]
and the positiveness of
\Theta_{\textup{m}}
give:

(4.13)

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{T}_{\ell}\Xi,\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}\geq\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\|\Xi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}((\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\Xi)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\nabla\Xi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x+\dfrac{\|\{\Xi\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}+\dfrac{\|\{\Xi-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\|\Xi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}((\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\Xi)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\nabla\Xi|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\dfrac{\|\{\Xi\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}+\dfrac{\|\{\Xi-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}}{2\min\left\{\dfrac{\rho c}{k},\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\right\}}\int_{\Omega}|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x-\dfrac{\min\left\{\dfrac{\rho c}{k},\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\right\}}{2}\|\Xi\|_{V}^{2},

where
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{Ctr}{{{}}\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}}>0
is the
continuity constant of the trace operator
\textup{tr}:V\to\Omega\times\{0\}
(cf., e.g., Chapter 18 in
[
52
]
).

An application of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Lemma
4.21
and (
4.12
) gives:

(4.14)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}((\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\Xi)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq-\sqrt{2}L\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}\|\nabla\Xi\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\geq-\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\Xi\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

Combining (
4.13
) and (
4.14
) gives

\langle\tilde{T}_{\ell}\Xi,\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\min\left\{\dfrac{\rho c}{k},\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\right\}\|\Xi\|_{V}^{2}-\dfrac{\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}}{2\min\left\{\dfrac{\rho c}{k},\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\right\}}\int_{\Omega}|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

so that
\langle\tilde{T}_{\ell}\Xi,\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}/\|\Xi\|_{V}\to\infty
as
\|\Xi\|_{V}\to\infty
and the sought coerciveness is established. The latter conclusion and the fact that
h_{\ell,k}^{n}\in V^{\prime}
(Lemma
4.22
) put us in position to apply the Minty–Browder theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 12.5-3 in
[
21
]
) so as to infer the existence of a unique element
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\in V
satisfying (
4.11
).

(ii)

The variational equations (
4.10
) admit a unique solution
u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\in W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
.
Observe that, thanks to item
(i)
, the existence and uniqueness for
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}
has already been established.
This puts us in position to consider the operator
A_{\ell}:W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\to W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
defined by:

A_{\ell}(v):=\dfrac{|v|^{\alpha-2}v}{k}-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})\nabla\cdot\left(|\nabla v|^{p-2}\nabla v\right)-\dfrac{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}{\ell}\{v\}^{-},\quad\textup{ for all }v\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega).

The operator
A_{\ell}
is hemi-continuous, as each of its terms is hemi-continuous. By Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.7
, the operator
A_{\ell}
is strictly monotone.
Finally, an application of the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.3-3
(a)
in
[
21
]
) and the monotonicity of the negative part operator (Lemma
4.1
) give:

\dfrac{\langle A_{\ell}v,v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}}{\|v\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}}\geq\dfrac{k^{-1}\|v\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}+\mu_{1}\|\nabla v\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}^{p}}{\|v\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}}\geq\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{cP}{{{}}c_{P}}^{p}}\|v\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p-1}=\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p}}\|v\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p-1}.

Observing that the term on the right diverges as
\|v\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\to\infty
, we obtain that the operator
A_{\ell}
is coercive.
An application of the Minty–Browder theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 12.5-3 in
[
21
]
) ensures that the numerical scheme in (
4.10
) admits a unique solution
u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
for (
4.10
).

The proof is complete.
∎

Given an integer
N\in\mathbb{N}
, and recalling that
k=T/N
, define the mapping
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{right-shift-u}{{{}}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}}:(0,T)\to W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
by

(4.15)

\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t):=u_{\ell,k}^{n+1},\quad\textup{ if }nk<t\leq(n+1)k,

and define the mapping
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{right-shift-T}{{{}}\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}}:(0,T)\to V
by:

(4.16)

\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t):=\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1},\quad\textup{ if }nk<t\leq(n+1)k.

It is also helpful to consider the left shift by
k
of the mapping
\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}
, that we denote and define by

(4.17)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{left-shift-u}{{{}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}}(t):=\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t-k),\quad\textup{ if }nk<t\leq(n+1)k,

where we let
\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)=u_{0}
if
t\leq 0
.

In the same fashion, we denote and define the
left shift by
k
of the mapping
\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}
by

(4.18)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{left-shift-T}{{{}}\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}}(t):=\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t-k),\quad\textup{ if }nk<t\leq(n+1)k,

where we let
\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t)=\tilde{\Theta}_{0}
if
t\leq 0
.

Given
\bm{v}_{\ell,k}=\{v_{\ell,k}^{n}\}_{n=0}^{N}
, the function
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{Dk}{{{}}D_{k}}(\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{\ell,k}):(0,T)\to W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
is defined by

D_{k}(\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{\ell,k})(t):=\dfrac{v_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-v_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k},\quad\textup{ for all }nk<t\leq(n+1)k,\quad 0\leq n\leq N-1.

We now recall a variant of the Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness theorem, established by Dreher & Jüngel
[
28
]
, which is helpful for studying finite-difference equations.

Lemma 4.24

(Theorem 1 in
[
28
]
)
.

Let
X_{0}
,
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
be Banach spaces such that
X_{0}\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow X_{1}\hookrightarrow X_{2}
, let
0<T<\infty
and let
N\geq 1
be an arbitrary integer. Let either
1\leq p<\infty
and
r=1
, or
p=\infty
and
r>1
. Let
k:=T/N
and consider the vector
\bm{v}_{k}:=\{v_{k}^{n}\}_{n=0}^{N}
of elements of
X_{0}
, and define the function
\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}:(0,T)\to X_{0}
by:

(\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k})(t):=v_{k}^{n+1},\quad\textup{ for a.a. }nk<t\leq(n+1)k.

Assume that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
k
such that:

k\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|\dfrac{v_{k}^{n+1}-v_{k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{X_{2}}^{r}+k\sum_{n=0}^{N}\|v_{k}^{n}\|_{X_{0}}^{p}\leq C.

Then, it results that:

(a)

If
1\leq p<\infty
, the sequence
\{\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is relatively compact in
L^{p}(0,T;X_{1})
;

(b)

If
p=\infty
, the sequence
\{\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is relatively compact in
L^{q}(0,T;X_{1})
for each
1\leq q<\infty
and the limit belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];X_{1})
.

∎

We also provide the statement for the discrete version of Lemma
4.20
, originally established by P.-A. Raviart in
[
64
]
and then refined by Chen, Jüngel & Liu in
[
19
]
.

Lemma 4.25

(Lemma 1.4 of
[
65
]
and Theorem 2 in
[
19
]
)
.

Let
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
be Banach spaces such that
A_{0}\hookrightarrow A_{1}
, let
0<T<\infty
and let
N\geq 1
be an arbitrary integer.
Let
S\subset A_{0}
be such that
\lambda S\subset S
, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.
Assume that the set
S
is endowed with the definite homogeneous gauge
M:S\to\mathbb{R}
, having the following properties:

•

M(v)\geq 0
, for all
v\in S
,

•

M(\lambda v)=|\lambda|M(v)
, for all
v\in S
and all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.

Assume that the set
\mathscr{M}:=\{v\in S;M(v)\leq 1\}
is relatively compact in
A_{0}
.
Let
k:=T/N
, and let
\bm{v}_{k}:=\{v_{k}^{n}\}_{n=0}^{N}
be a vector of elements in
S
. Assume that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
N
such that

k\sum_{n=0}^{N}[M(v_{k}^{n})]^{q_{0}}\leq C,

and

k\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|\dfrac{v_{k}^{n+1}-v_{k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{A_{1}}^{q_{1}}\leq C,

where
1\leq q_{0}\leq\infty
and
1\leq q_{1}\leq\infty
, and the pairs
(q_{0},q_{1})=(1,\infty)
and
(q_{0},q_{1})=(\infty,1)
cannot be attained. Define the function
\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}:(0,T)\to A_{0}
by:

(\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k})(t):=v_{k}^{n+1},\quad\textup{ for a.a. }nk<t\leq(n+1)k.

Then, it results that:

(a)

If
1\leq q_{0}<\infty
, the sequence
\{\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is relatively compact in
L^{q_{0}}(0,T;A_{0})
;

(b)

If
q_{0}=\infty
, the sequence
\{\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is relatively compact in
L^{q}(0,T;A_{0})
for each
1\leq q<\infty
and the limit belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];A_{0})
.

∎

Remark 4.26
.

The conclusion of Lemma
4.24
and Lemma
4.25
also holds for the left shift of the piecewise constant-in-time functions
\Pi_{k}\bm{v}_{k}
, upon considering a suitable extension (viz., e.g., (
4.17
) and (
4.18
)) (cf., e.g., Theorem 2 in
[
19
]
).

\blacksquare

5.
A priori estimates

In this section we discuss the convergence of the sequences
\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
and
\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
as
N\to\infty
. To this aim, we need to establish some
a priori
estimates. While the recovery of the energy estimates for (
4.10
) follows the strategy of Theorem 4.2 in
[
63
]
, the presence of the temperature in (
4.10
) and the form of the equations (
4.11
) require additional care.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
T>0
,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
p
be as in Section
4
and let
\alpha
be as in (
4.1
). Let
\ell>0
be given and intended to go to zero, let
N\geq 1
be an integer, and define
k:=T/N
.
Assume that (
H
1
)–(
H
3
) hold and that the positive constant
L
satisfies the upper bound (
4.12
).

Then, it results:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\left\{k\sum_{n=0}^{N}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\{k\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|\dfrac{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\\
&\qquad\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,\end{aligned}

(5.5)

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N-1}\left\|\dfrac{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\\
&\qquad\textup{ is bounded independently of }N,\end{aligned}

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left\{k\sum_{n=0}^{N}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{V}^{2}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{V}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\left\{k\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell.

Proof.

The proof is divided into seven steps, numbered
(i)
–
(vii)
.

(i)

Proof of (
5.1
) and (
5.2
).

Multiply (
4.10
) by
u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}
in the sense of the duality between
W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
and
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
, obtaining:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{\Omega}\left(|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Using the first estimate in Lemma
4.7
with
r=\alpha
,
\xi=u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}
and
\eta=u_{\ell,k}^{n}
, the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.3-3
(a)
in
[
21
]
), Bochner’s theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.9 of
[
52
]
), Hölder’s inequality, Young’s inequality (cf., e.g.,
[
73
]
and page 92 in
[
11
]
), the fact that
p>2
, and (
2.21
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}k}(\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}-\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha})+\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p}}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{\mu_{1}}{\ell}\|\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}k^{p^{\prime}/p}\left(\dfrac{1}{k^{p^{\prime}}}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\|\tilde{a}(t)\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{p^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)+\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}.

Multiplying both sides of the latter estimate by
(k\alpha^{\prime})
, and summing over
0\leq n\leq s-1
, where
1\leq s\leq N
, gives

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left\{\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}-\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}+\dfrac{\alpha^{\prime}k\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{\alpha^{\prime}k\mu_{1}}{\ell}\|\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\prime}\left(\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left(\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\|\tilde{a}(t)\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{p^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right),

and we finally obtain:

\displaystyle\|u_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}+\dfrac{\alpha^{\prime}k\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{\alpha^{\prime}k\mu_{1}}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\|\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{\prime}\left(\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left(\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\|\tilde{a}(t)\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{p^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)+\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha},

that establishes (
5.1
) and (
5.2
). In particular, the estimates (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) and the fact that
u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}
imply that there exists a constant independent of
N
and
\ell
such that:

(5.12)

\displaystyle\dfrac{k}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\|\{u_{\ell,k}^{n}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq\dfrac{1}{\mu_{1}}\left(\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left(\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\|\tilde{a}(t)\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{p^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\mu_{1}\alpha^{\prime}}.

For the sake of brevity, we denote by
C_{1}(k)
the following constant:

(5.13)

C_{1}(k):=\dfrac{1}{\mu_{1}}\left(\dfrac{\mu_{1}p}{2(p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p})}\right)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left(\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\|\tilde{a}(t)\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{p^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)+\dfrac{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\mu_{1}\alpha^{\prime}}.

(ii)
Proof of (
5.3
)
.
By (
5.1
), for each
0\leq n\leq N
, we have that:

\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}=\int_{\Omega}\left||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

Since the left-hand side of the previous identity is bounded independently of
N
and
\ell
, we immediately infer that the sequence

\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty},

is bounded by
C_{1}
defined in (
5.13
), thus establishing the estimate (
5.3
).

(iii)
Proof of (
5.8
) and (
5.9
)
. Multiply (
4.11
) by
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}
in the sense of the duality between
V^{\prime}
and
V
, obtaining:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\left((\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\right)\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\dfrac{\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}A(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x.

An application of the Young inequality
[
73
]
, Lemma
4.21
, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality
[
11
]
, (
2.10
), (
4.12
), assumption
(H\ref{H4})
, the positiveness of
\Theta_{\textup{m}}
the Sobolev embedding theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.4-1 in
[
21
]
), Lemma
4.4
, estimate (
5.6
) to the equation above gives:

(5.14)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{2k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{\rho c}{2k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{\rho c}{2k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{\rho c}{2k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\kappa-\rho c\sqrt{2}L\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}\right)\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}+\dfrac{\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}}{\ell}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\Theta_{\textup{m}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{16L^{3}A_{0}^{2}(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}}{\kappa}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p-1}(\Omega)}^{p-1}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{2(1+2L^{2})}{\kappa}\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

As a result, we obtain the estimate:

(5.15)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{2k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{\rho c}{2k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{16L^{3}A_{0}^{2}(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}}{\kappa}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p-1}(\Omega)}^{p-1}+\dfrac{2(1+2L^{2})}{\kappa}\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

Multiply (
5.15
) by
k
and sum over
n=0,\dots,s-1
, for all
1\leq s\leq N
. The fact that
k:=T/N
and an application of Young’s inequality
[
73
]
, Hölder’s inequality and (
5.2
) give

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{2}\left(\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\|\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\right)+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\sum_{n=1}^{s}k\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{8Lc_{P,L}^{2}A_{0}^{2}(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}}{\kappa}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p-1}(\Omega)}^{p-1}+\dfrac{2(1+2L^{2})T}{\kappa}\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{16L^{3}|\Omega|^{1/p}A_{0}^{2}(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}}{\kappa p^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{16L^{3}|\Omega|^{1/p}A_{0}^{2}(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}T}{\kappa p}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{2(1+2L^{2})T}{\kappa}\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2},

and the estimate (
5.8
) is thus established. Estimate (
5.9
) immediately follows by Lemma
4.21
. In particular, from (
5.14
) and the estimates (
5.8
) and (
5.9
), we infer that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
N
and
\ell
such that:

(5.16)

\displaystyle\dfrac{k}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\leq C,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\dfrac{k}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\leq C.

(iv)
Proof of (
5.10
) and (
5.11
)
. Multiply (
4.11
) by
(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})
in the sense of the duality between
V^{\prime}
and
V
, obtaining:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\kappa\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\left((\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\right)(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}A(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z.

An application of Young’s inequality
[
73
]
allows us to estimate the previous identity as follows:

(5.18)

\displaystyle\rho ck\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle-\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}^{2}\left(k\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\right)-\dfrac{\rho ck}{4}\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}Lk}{\rho c}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p-1}(\Omega)}^{p-1}+\dfrac{\rho ck}{4}\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x.

Let us now estimate the penalty terms in (
5.18
). To begin with, we observe that an application of Young’s inequality
[
73
]
gives:

(5.19)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\left|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z-\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

Summing (
5.19
) over
n=0,\dots,s-1
, for all
1\leq s\leq N
gives

(5.20)

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}=\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\geq 0,

being
\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\in K_{\textup{temp}}
. For the second, and last, penalty term we observe that another application of the Young inequality
[
73
]
gives:

(5.21)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\left((\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}})-(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}-\Theta_{\textup{m}})\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

Summing (
5.21
) over
n=0,\dots,s-1
, for all
1\leq s\leq N
, and recalling that
\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\in K_{\textup{temp}}
gives:

(5.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{0}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}\geq 0.

Summing (
5.18
) over
n=0,\dots,s-1
, for all
1\leq s\leq N
, and applying the estimates (
5.20
) and (
5.22
) gives:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}-\dfrac{\kappa}{2}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}L}{\rho c}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p-1}(\Omega)}^{p-1}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}-\tilde{\Theta}_{0})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle+4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}L\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p-1}(\Omega)}^{p-1}+\dfrac{2\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}(1+2L^{2})}{\kappa}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}L|\Omega|^{1/p}}{p^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}L|\Omega|^{1/p}T}{p}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{2\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}(1+2L^{2})}{\kappa}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

A rearrangement in the chain of estimates above gives:

(5.23)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}+\dfrac{\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho c\|(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\|_{\bm{\mathcal{C}}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}\times[0,L])}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}L|\Omega|^{1/p}}{p^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{4(\rho gC_{0})^{2p}A_{0}^{2}L|\Omega|^{1/p}T}{p}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{2\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}^{2}(1+2L^{2})}{\kappa}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\dfrac{3\kappa}{4}\|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\|_{\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

Since, thanks to (
5.2
) and (
5.9
), the right-hand side of (
5.23
) is bounded independently of
N
, we infer that the estimates (
5.10
) and (
5.11
) hold upon applying Lemma
4.21
.

(v)
Proof of (
5.4
) and (
5.5
)
. Multiply (
4.10
) by
(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,k}^{n})
in the sense of the duality between
W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
and
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
and divide by
\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})>0
, obtaining:

(5.24)

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{k\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}\int_{\Omega}\left(|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right)(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x=\dfrac{1}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x.

For the sake of brevity, for each
0\leq n\leq N-1
define:

\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}:=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

For each
1\leq s\leq N
, the following identity holds:

(5.25)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,k}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n+1}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})}-\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{s-1}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s})}u_{\ell,k}^{s}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{0}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{1})}u_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\dfrac{1}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\tilde{a}_{k}^{n+1}-\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{s-1}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s})}u_{\ell,k}^{s}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{0}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{1})}u_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})}-\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x.

For what concerns the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.25
), the following estimate clearly holds thanks to Hölder’s inequality, assumption
(H\ref{H3})
and item
(i)
established beforehand:

(5.26)

\left|\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{s-1}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{s})}u_{\ell,k}^{s}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\dfrac{\|\tilde{a}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))}}{\mu_{1}}\left(\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}\right).

For what concerns the third term on the right-hand side of (
5.25
), the following estimate also holds thanks to Hölder’s inequality, assumption
(H\ref{H3})
and (
5.1
) established beforehand:

(5.27)

\left|\int_{\Omega}\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{0}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{1})}u_{0}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\dfrac{\|\tilde{a}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))}}{\mu_{1}}\left(\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}\right).

For what concerns the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.25
), observe that for each
n=0,\dots,s-2
the following identity holds in
\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
:

\displaystyle\tilde{a}_{k}^{n+1}-\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}(\tilde{a}(t+k)-\tilde{a}(t))\,\mathrm{d}t=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{t}^{t+k}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}s}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{nk}^{s}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}s}(s)\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s+\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{(n+1)k}^{(n+2)k}\int_{s-k}^{(n+1)k}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}s}(s)\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}s}(s)(s-nk)\,\mathrm{d}s+\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{(n+1)k}^{(n+2)k}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}s}(s)((n+2)k-s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Taking the norm
\|\cdot\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}
in the identity above and exploiting
(H\ref{H3})
gives:

\|\tilde{a}_{k}^{n+1}-\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq 2k\left\|\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}.

Another application of
(H\ref{H3})
and of the estimate above allows us to obtain the following estimate for the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.25
):

(5.28)

\left|\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\dfrac{1}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})}\int_{\Omega}\left(\tilde{a}_{k}^{n+1}-\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\leq\dfrac{2k}{\mu_{1}}\left\|\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}.

An application of Young’s inequality
[
73
]
to the right-hand side of the estimate above gives

(5.29)

\displaystyle k\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\leq\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\left\{\dfrac{k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}}{p}+\dfrac{k}{p^{\prime}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{k}{p}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}+\dfrac{T}{p^{\prime}},

and, thanks to (
5.2
) and (
5.29
), we obtain that

(5.30)

\left\{k\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ is bounded independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

thus showing that (
5.28
) is bounded independently of
N
and
\ell
.

Finally, we estimate the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.25
). To begin with, let us observe that for each
n=0,\dots,N-1
, an application of Lemma
4.1
(a)
, the Mean Value Theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 9.3-1 in
[
21
]
) and Hölder’s inequality give:

(5.31)

\displaystyle|\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n})|

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{0}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg|\exp\left(-\dfrac{\beta\sqrt{L}}{|\Omega|^{1/2}}\int_{\Omega}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}(x,s^{\prime}L)-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)

\displaystyle-\exp\left(-\dfrac{\beta\sqrt{L}}{|\Omega|^{1/2}}\int_{\Omega}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}(x,s^{\prime}L)-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)\bigg|\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{0}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}\dfrac{\beta}{\sqrt{L|\Omega|}}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{-}-\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{-}\right|\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{0}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}\dfrac{\beta}{\sqrt{L|\Omega|}}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}|\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq 2A_{0}\beta\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}.

An application of Young’s inequality
[
73
]
and (
5.31
) give:

(5.32)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})}-\dfrac{\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\|\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\left|\dfrac{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+2})\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{2A_{0}\beta}{\mu_{1}^{2}}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}\|\tilde{a}_{k}^{n}\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{4\beta^{2}A_{0}^{2}}{\rho c\mu_{1}^{3}}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{2p-2}\|\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\rho c}{4\mu_{1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{8\beta^{2}A_{0}^{2}}{p\rho c\mu_{1}^{3}}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{2p-2}\|\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{4(p-2)\beta^{2}A_{0}^{2}T}{p\rho c\mu_{1}^{3}}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{2p-2}\|\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{\rho c}{4\mu_{1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

For any
0\leq n\leq N-1
, we have that an application of the Young’s inequality
[
73
]
gives:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}(u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x=\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left(-\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}-\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\left(\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n}\}^{+}-\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n}\}^{-}\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n}\}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}+\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

For any given
1\leq s\leq N
, we thus have that the previous estimate gives:

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}(u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\leq-\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}\right|^{2}-\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{s}\}^{-}\right|^{2}-\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{0}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=-\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{s}\}^{-}\right|^{2}-\left|\{u_{0}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2\ell}\int_{\Omega}\left|\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{s}\}^{-}\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 0,

where the last equality directly follows from the fact that
u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}
.
To summarise, we have shown that:

(5.33)

\dfrac{1}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}\}^{-}(u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n+1}-u_{\ell,\kappa}^{n})\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 0.

Summing (
5.24
) over
0\leq n\leq s-1
, with
1\leq s\leq N
, applying Lemma
4.7
, exploiting (
5.26
)–(
5.32
), (
5.33
), Young’s inequality
[
73
]
, the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality (cf., e.g. Theorem 8.3-3
(a)
in
[
21
]
) and (
2.21
) gives:

\displaystyle\dfrac{4(\alpha-1)k}{\alpha^{2}\mu_{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left\|\dfrac{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{p+(\textup{diam }\Omega)^{p}}\left(\|u_{\ell,k}^{s}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}-\|u_{0}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{4(\alpha-1)k}{\alpha^{2}\mu_{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left\|\dfrac{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{p}\sum_{n=0}^{s-1}\left\{\|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}^{p}-\|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}^{p}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\dfrac{2\|\tilde{a}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))}}{\mu_{1}}\left(\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle+\dfrac{2}{\mu_{1}p}\left\|\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{a}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}\left(\dfrac{pT}{p^{\prime}}+\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}\right)

\displaystyle+\dfrac{8\beta^{2}A_{0}^{2}}{p\rho c\mu_{1}^{3}}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{2p-2}\|\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{p}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{4(p-2)\beta^{2}A_{0}^{2}T}{p\rho c\mu_{1}^{3}}\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{2p-2}\|\tilde{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))}^{2}+\dfrac{\rho c}{4\mu_{1}}\sum_{n=0}^{s-2}k\left\|\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))}^{2}.

An application of (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and (
5.11
) in turn implies the validity of estimates (
5.4
) and (
5.5
).

(vi)
Proof of (
5.6
)
. In order to establish the estimate (
5.6
), we exploit the boundedness of the
p
-Laplace operator (cf., e.g., Section 12.6 in
[
21
]
), the validity of the embedding
L^{\alpha}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, and (
5.5
) so as to be in a position to evaluate

(5.34)

\displaystyle\left\|\dfrac{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\right\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{2}\left\|\nabla\cdot\left(|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\right)\right\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}+\dfrac{\mu_{2}}{\ell}\|\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}+\|\tilde{a}\|_{W^{1,p}(0,T;\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{\Omega}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\right),\quad\textup{ for some }C>0\textup{ independent of }N,

for all
0\leq n\leq N-1
, thus establishing the estimate (
5.6
).

(vii)
Proof of (
5.7
)
. By (
5.1
), for each
0\leq n\leq N
, we have that:

\displaystyle\|u_{\ell,k}^{n}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}=\left(\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/\alpha}=\left(\int_{\Omega}\left||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/\alpha}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\Omega}\left||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right|^{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}(\alpha-1)}}=\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{\alpha^{\prime}/\alpha}.

Since the first term is bounded independently of
N
and
\ell
, we immediately infer that the sequence

(5.35)

\left\{\max_{0\leq n\leq N}\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty},

is bounded independently of
N
and
\ell
, thus establishing (
5.7
). The proof is complete.
∎

5.1
can be summarised by means of the following diagram.

Figure 2
.
Dependency graph for the proof of Theorem
5.1
. Solid arrows indicate which estimate is proved by each step; dashed arrows indicate which previously proved estimate is used as an input.

For each
\ell>0
, define the operator
B_{\ell}:W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\to W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
by

(5.36)

\langle B_{\ell}(u),v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}:=\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x,

for all
u,v\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
.

The
a priori
estimates (
5.1
)–(
5.11
) can be summarised as follows:

(5.37)

\displaystyle\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{D_{k}(|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{D_{k}(|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ independently of }N,

\displaystyle\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{-\Delta_{b}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V^{\prime})\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{B_{\ell}(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell,

\displaystyle\{D_{k}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\textup{ is bounded in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ independently of }N\textup{ and }\ell.

Observe, in particular, that the third last boundedness derives from the fact that, for each
\Xi\in V
such that
\|\Xi\|_{V}=1
, the following estimates hold for a.a.
t\in(0,T)

\left|\langle-\Delta_{b}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t)),\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}\right|\leq\|\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}+\hat{C}_{\textup{tr}}\|q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

while the second last boundedness descends from (
5.34
).

6.
Other preliminary lemmas

Let us now show that, thanks to the discrete version of Dubinskii’s lemma (Lemma
4.25
) applies to the sequence
\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
. As an application of the Banach–Alaoglu–Bourbaki theorem (cf.,e.g., Theorem 3.6 of
[
11
]
), up to passing to a suitable subsequence, it results
\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}u_{\ell}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))
as
N\to\infty
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Consider the sequence
\{\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
in (
4.17
). Then, up to passing to a suitable subsequence, it results that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}u_{\ell},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\text{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\to|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{q_{0}}(0,T;L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty\textup{ for all }1\leq q_{0}<\infty.

Proof.

By virtue of (
5.37
) and the fact that
\Lambda_{k}
differs from
\Pi_{k}
only by a time translation with time-step
k
, the sequence
\{\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))
, and this bound is independent of
k
. Recall that the sequence
\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
independently of
k
, and that the sequence
\left\{D_{k}\left(|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\right)\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
independently of
k
. Consequently, by extracting a suitable subsequence, we obtain

(6.1)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}u_{\ell},

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\text{ as }N\to\infty,
