\displaystyle|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}v_{\ell},

\displaystyle\text{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\text{ as }N\to\infty,

and the first claimed convergence is thus established.

The rest of the proof, devoted to the identification of the limit in the second convergence of (
6.1
), is divided into three parts, numbered
(i)
–
(iii)
.

(i)

Construction of a semi-normed cone
. For any
u\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
, we seek a real number
\beta
such that:

\left||u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u\right|^{\frac{\beta-2}{2}}|u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u=u.

We note that a sufficient condition for this is that
\beta
satisfies

\left(\dfrac{\alpha-2}{2}+1\right)\dfrac{\beta-2}{2}+\dfrac{\alpha-2}{2}=0,

which is equivalent to

\beta=2+2\left(\dfrac{2-\alpha}{2}\dfrac{2}{\alpha}\right)=\dfrac{4}{\alpha}.

Let
v:=|u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u
and observe that if
\beta=4/\alpha
, then
|v|^{\frac{\beta-2}{2}}v\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
. Hence, the set

\tilde{S}:=\{v;(|v|^{\frac{(4/\alpha)-2}{2}}v)\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\}

is a non-empty cone. Define the function

\tilde{M}(v):=\left\|\nabla(|v|^{\frac{(4/\alpha)-2}{2}}v)\right\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}=\left\|\nabla(|v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v)\right\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}},\quad\text{ for all }v\in\tilde{S}.

We need to check that
\tilde{M}
is a definite homogeneous gauge. Clearly, the function
\tilde{M}
is non-negative and if
v=0
then
\tilde{M}(v)=0
.
If
\tilde{M}(v)=0
, an application of the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality gives

0=\tilde{M}(v)\geq c_{P}^{-\alpha/2}\left\||v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}^{\alpha/2},

and the latter in turn implies that
|v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v=0
in
L^{p}(\Omega)
. The definition of
\alpha
in turn gives that

\dfrac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}=\dfrac{p+1}{3p-1}>0,

which implies
v=0
as it was to be proved. Finally, let
\lambda\geq 0
and let
v\in\tilde{S}
and note that a straightforward computation leads to
\tilde{M}(\lambda v)=\lambda\tilde{M}(v)
.

(ii)

A pre-compactness argument
. Define the set

\tilde{\mathscr{M}}:=\{v\in\tilde{S};\tilde{M}(v)\leq 1\}.

An application of the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.3-3
(a)
in
[
21
]
) gives:

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{M}(v)\geq(1+c_{P}^{p})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2p}}\left\||v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\right\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\geq(1+c_{P}^{p})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2p}}\left\||v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}

\displaystyle=(1+c_{P}^{p})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2p}}\left(\int_{\Omega}\left||v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\alpha/(2p)}=(1+c_{P}^{p})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2p}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|v|^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\alpha/(2p)}=(1+c_{P}^{p})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2p}}\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)}.

Let
\{v_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence in
\tilde{\mathscr{M}}
. By the Rellich–Kondrašov theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.4-3 in
[
21
]
), we have
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
. Thus, up to passing to a subsequence, there exists
w\in L^{p}(\Omega)
such that:

(6.2)

(|v_{m}|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v_{m})\to w,\quad\text{ in }L^{p}(\Omega)\text{ as }m\to\infty.

Given that
1<\alpha<2
and
2.8\leq p\leq 5
, it follows that
1\leq p^{\prime}<2<p<\frac{2p}{\alpha}<2p<\infty
and that
\{v_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
. The reflexivity of
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
allows us to apply the Banach–Eberlein–Smulian theorem (see, e.g., Theorem 6.17-8 of
[
21
]
) and extract a subsequence, still denoted
\{v_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}
, that converges weakly to an element
v
in
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
as
m\to\infty
. Consider the mapping:

v\in L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)\mapsto(|v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v)\in L^{\left(\frac{2p}{\alpha}\right)^{\prime}}(\Omega).

This mapping is hemi-continuous and monotone because the function
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\to(|\xi|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
is continuous and monotone. Therefore, by Theorem 12.5-2
(a)
of
[
21
]
, we have
w=|v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\in L^{\left(\frac{2p}{\alpha}\right)^{\prime}}(\Omega)
. Hence, the convergence in (
6.2
) and the uniqueness of the limit gives:

(6.3)

(|v_{m}|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v_{m})\to w=(|v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v),\quad\text{ in }L^{p}(\Omega)\text{ as }m\to\infty.

To show that
\tilde{\mathscr{M}}
is relatively compact in
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
, we must show that every sequence
\{v_{m}\}_{m=1}^{\infty}\subset\tilde{\mathscr{M}}
has a convergent subsequence in
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
.

Since
2p/\alpha>1
, Lemma
4.3
gives:

\displaystyle\left|\|v_{m}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)}-\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)}\right|=\left|\left(\int_{\Omega}|v_{m}|^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2p}}-\left(\int_{\Omega}|v|^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2p}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{\Omega}|v_{m}|^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|v|^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{2p}}=\left|\int_{\Omega}\left||v_{m}|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v_{m}\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}\left||v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{2p}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left\||v_{m}|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v_{m}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}^{p}-\left\||v|^{\frac{2-\alpha}{\alpha}}v\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}^{p}\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{2p}}.

From (
6.3
), the right-hand side tends to zero as
m\to\infty
, establishing that

\|v_{m}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)}\to\|v\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)},\quad\text{ as }m\to\infty.

Since
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
is uniformly convex, Theorem 5.15-3 of
[
21
]
implies that

v_{m}\to v,\quad\textup{ in }L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)\textup{ as }m\to\infty,

which establishes the desired relative compactness.

(iii)

Application of Lemma
4.25
and limit identification.
Since
\tilde{\mathscr{M}}
is relatively compact in
L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
, since

\tilde{M}\bigl(|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\bigr)=\|\nabla\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}^{\alpha/2}

is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T)
independently of
k
, and since the sequence
\bigl\{D_{k}\bigl(|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\bigr)\bigr\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
independently of
k
, we apply Dubinskii’s lemma for finite difference quotients (Lemma
4.25
) with
A_{0}=L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
,
A_{1}=L^{2}(\Omega)
,
q_{0}\in[1,\infty)
, and
q_{1}=2
. Therefore, the following convergence holds:

(6.4)

|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\to v_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{q_{0}}(0,T;L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty.

We now identify the limit
v_{\ell}
. Define the Nemytskii operator
\hat{A}:L^{p}((0,T)\times\Omega)\to L^{p^{\prime}}((0,T)\times\Omega)
by

\hat{A}(w):=|w|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}w,\quad\textup{ for all }w\in L^{p}((0,T)\times\Omega).

For
w\in L^{p}((0,T)\times\Omega)
we have
|\hat{A}(w)|^{p^{\prime}}=|w|^{\alpha p^{\prime}/2}
. Thanks to the definition of
\alpha
(viz. (
4.1
)), the inequality
\frac{\alpha p^{\prime}}{2}\leq p
reduces to
4p^{2}-7p+1\geq 0
, which is satisfied for
2.8\leq p\leq 5
. Hence, it results that
\hat{A}(w)\in L^{p^{\prime}}((0,T)\times\Omega)
, and
\hat{A}
is well defined.

Since
2p/\alpha\geq p^{\prime}
(equivalent, again, to
4p^{2}-7p+1\geq 0
), since
\Omega
is bounded, and since
0<T<\infty
, the following continuous embedding holds:

L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}((0,T)\times\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p^{\prime}}((0,T)\times\Omega).

Choosing
q_{0}=p^{\prime}
in (
6.4
), an application of Theorem 8.28 in
[
52
]
gives that the previous strong convergence also holds in
L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
:

\hat{A}(\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\to v_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty.

The scalar function
\xi\mapsto|\xi|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\xi
is continuous and strictly increasing, therefore the associated Nemytskii operator
A
is monotone and hemi-continuous from
L^{p}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega))
to its dual
L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
. From (
6.1
) we have
\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\rightharpoonup u_{\ell}
in
L^{p}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega))
as
N\to\infty
. Applying Theorem 12.5-2
(a)
of
[
21
]
with
X=L^{p}(0,T;L^{p}(\Omega))
and
X^{\prime}=L^{p^{\prime}}(0,T;L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
yields

v_{\ell}=\hat{A}(u_{\ell})=|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell},

and the proof is complete.
∎

Thanks to Lemma
6.1
, we can apply Lemma
4.10
and Lemma
4.11
, and establish that:

(6.5)

\left\{\left(\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\textup{ converges strongly in }L^{\frac{2p}{p-1}}((0,T)\times\Omega\times(0,L)).

We observe that, according to (
5.37
) it is possible to extract a subsequence such that:

\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\textup{ as }N\to\infty.

We have already established in (
5.37
) that
\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;V)
independently of
N
and that
\{D_{k}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
independently of
N
and
\ell
. Thanks to the Rellich–Kondrašov theorem, the dense and compact embedding
V\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L))
holds for all
2\leq q<6=2^{\ast}
. Therefore, the following chain of embeddings holds:

(6.6)

V\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow V^{\prime}.

An application of the Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness theorem for finite difference quotients in time (Lemma
4.24
) gives that, up to passing to subsequences, the following convergences hold for all
2\leq q<6
:

(6.7)

\displaystyle\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\to\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}

\displaystyle,\quad\textup{ in }L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\to\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}

\displaystyle,\quad\textup{ in }L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }N\to\infty.

The Nemitskii operator associated with the operator
A
introduced in (
2.10
) is denoted and defined by:

(6.8)

\mathcal{A}(\Xi)(t):=A(t,x,\Xi(t)),\quad\textup{ for all }\Xi\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{1}(\Omega\times(0,L))).

Let us now discuss the regularity and continuity of the operators
\mathcal{A}
and
\mu
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
0<T<\infty
, let
\Omega
be a Lipschitz domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and let
0<L<\infty
. Assume that
1\leq q<\infty
. Then, the operator
\mathcal{A}
introduced in (
6.8
) and the operator
\mu
defined in (
4.7
) satisfy the following properties:

(a)

\mathcal{A}(\Xi)\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
, for all
\Xi\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
;

(b)

\mathcal{A}
is continuous from
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
to
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
;

(c)

\mathcal{A}
is continuous from
L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
to
L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
;

(d)

\mu(\Xi)\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T])
, for all
\Xi\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
;

(e)

\mu
is continuous from
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
to
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T])
;

(f)

\mu
is continuous from
L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
to
L^{q}(0,T)
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into four steps, numbered
(i)
–
(iv)
.

(i)

Proof of properties
(a)
and
(b)
. Observe that the operator
\mathcal{A}
is composition of the affine shift
\Xi\mapsto(\Xi-\Theta_{\textup{m}})
, the operator
(-\{\cdot\}^{-})
(that is Lipschitz continuous up to a change of sign by Lemma
4.1
), multiplication by
-\beta
, the exponential (which is continuous by Lemma
4.14
, noting the input is non-positive), multiplication by
A_{0}
as well as the Lipschitz continuous mapping
f\mapsto\max\{f,A_{\textup{min}}\}
.
Since each link in the composition preserves the
\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
regularity, properties
(a)
and
(b)
immediately follow.

(ii)

Proof of property
(c)
. Let
\{\Xi_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence such that
\Xi_{n}\to\Xi
in
L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
as
n\to\infty
. The definition of the operator
A
in (
2.10
), Lemma
4.1
(a)
and (
4.5
) in Lemma
4.13
give:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\mathcal{A}(\Xi_{n})-\mathcal{A}(\Xi)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq A_{0}^{q}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\left|\exp\left(-\beta\{\Xi_{n}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{-}\right)-\exp\left(-\beta\{\Xi-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{-}\right)\right|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq A_{0}^{q}\beta^{q}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\Xi_{n}-\Xi|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

and we observe that the assumed convergence for the sequence
\{\Xi_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
implies that the latter term tends to zero as
n\to\infty
.

(iii)

Proof of properties
(d)
and
(e)
. Properties
(d)
and
(e)
immediately follow as a result of an application of Lemma
4.16
and Remark
4.17
.

(iv)

Proof of property
(f)
. Let
\{\Xi_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence such that
\Xi_{n}\to\Xi
in
L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
as
n\to\infty
. The definition of
\mu
given in (
4.7
), the non-expansiveness of
\{\cdot\}^{+}
and
\{\cdot\}^{-}
, and the fact that the best Lipschitz constant for
\exp
with negative inputs is
1
give:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\mu(\Xi_{n})-\mu(\Xi)|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\left(2\left(\rho g\dfrac{p-1}{2p}\right)^{p-1}A_{0}\beta\right)^{q}(L|\Omega|)^{\frac{q-2}{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}|\Xi_{n}-\Xi|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

The assumed convergence for the sequence
\{\Xi_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
gives that the right-hand side of the estimate above tends to zero as
n\to\infty
.
∎

We are finally able to establish the strong convergence of the right-hand side of (
4.11
) in a suitable Bochner space.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
0<T<\infty
, let
\Omega
be a Lipschitz domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and let
0<L<\infty
. Assume that
\frac{10}{3}\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
, and that
2.8\leq p\leq 5
. Let
\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}
and
\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}
be the piecewise constant functions introduced in (
4.17
) and (
4.18
), respectively. Let
\sigma>0
be defined in a way that:

\dfrac{1}{\sigma}=\dfrac{1}{q}+\dfrac{p-1}{2p}.

Then, it results that
1\leq q^{\prime}\leq\sigma\leq 2
, and that the sequence
\{\Lambda_{k}\bm{h}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
with elements

\Lambda_{k}\bm{h}_{\ell,k}:=\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\left(\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}

admits a subsequence that converges strongly to
h_{\ell}:=\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}
in
L^{\sigma}(0,T;L^{\sigma}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

To begin with, observe that if
2.8\leq p\leq 5
and
\frac{10}{3}\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
, an application of Lemma
4.12
gives
q^{\prime}\leq\sigma\leq 2
and
2\leq\sigma^{\prime}\leq q
.
Additionally, again thanks to Lemma
4.12
, the chain of embeddings (
6.6
) can be improved as follows:

(6.9)

\displaystyle V\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow L^{\sigma^{\prime}}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))

\displaystyle\hookrightarrow L^{\sigma}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow L^{q^{\prime}}(\Omega\times(0,L))\hookrightarrow\hookrightarrow V^{\prime}.

By the Aubin–Lions–Simon theorem, up to passing to suitable subsequences with common indices, we obtain that

\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\to\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L))),

as
N\to\infty
and that (
6.5
) holds.

An application of the generalised Hölder inequality (cf., e.g., Remark 2 on page 93 of
[
11
]
), (
6.5
), Lemma
6.2
(c)
and Theorem 8.28 in
[
52
]
give

\displaystyle\left(\int_{(0,T)\times\Omega\times(0,L)}\left|\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\left(\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}-\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\right|^{\sigma}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\|_{L^{q}((0,T)\times\Omega\times(0,L))}\left\|\left(\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}-\left(\left\{|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-1}}((0,T)\times\Omega\times(0,L))}

\displaystyle+\|\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})-\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|_{L^{q}((0,T)\times\Omega\times(0,L))}\left\|\left(\left\{|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-1}}((0,T)\times\Omega\times(0,L))},

and we observe that the addends on the right-hand side tend to zero as
N\to\infty
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Thanks to (
6.9
) and the conclusion in Theorem
6.3
, we infer that the sequence

\left\{\mathcal{A}(\Lambda_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\left(\left\{|\Lambda_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}\right\}_{N=1}^{\infty}

admits a subsequence that strongly converges to
\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\left(\left\{|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}
in
L^{\sigma}(0,T;V^{\prime})
as
N\to\infty
.

\blacksquare

7.
Existence of weak solutions for Problem

P
ℓ

Thanks to (
5.37
), we can establish the existence of solutions for Problem

P
ℓ

.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
T>0
,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
p
be as in Section
4
and let
\alpha
be as in (
4.1
). Let
\ell>0
be given, let
N\geq 1
be an integer, and define
k:=T/N
.
Assume that
(H\ref{H1})
–
(H\ref{H4})
hold.
The a priori estimates (
5.37
) imply that the following convergence process takes place (recall that
B_{\ell}
has been defined in (
5.36
))

(7.1)

\displaystyle\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}u_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}v_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle D_{k}(|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}v_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}w_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle D_{k}(|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}w_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle|u_{\ell,k}^{N}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{N}\rightharpoonup\chi_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle B_{\ell}(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}g_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle-\Delta_{b}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}-\Delta_{b}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V^{\prime})\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{N}\rightharpoonup\tilde{\tau}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }V\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

\displaystyle D_{k}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\rightharpoonup\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

and the weak-star limits
g_{\ell}
,
v_{\ell}
, and
w_{\ell}
satisfy:

\displaystyle\langle g_{\ell},v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\ell}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell}\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x,

\displaystyle v_{\ell}

\displaystyle=|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell},

\displaystyle w_{\ell}

\displaystyle=|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell},

\displaystyle\chi_{\ell}

\displaystyle=|u_{\ell}(T)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(T),

\displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(T).

Besides, the pair
(u_{\ell},\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})
is a solution for Problem

P
ℓ

.

Proof.

The proof is divided into five steps, numbered
(i)
–
(v)
. The discussion of the thermal component of the model is novel and requires substantial care.

The convergences in (
7.1
) hold by virtue of the Banach–Alaoglu–Bourbaki theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 3.6 of
[
11
]
), the estimates (
5.37
) and (
5.35
).
The non-trivial part of the proof amounts to identifying the weak-star limits
v_{\ell}
and
w_{\ell}
, as well as to show that the weak-star limits
u_{\ell}
and
\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}
solve Problem

P
ℓ

.

(i)

It results that
w_{\ell}=|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}
. Given
u\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
, set
v:=|u|^{\alpha-2}u
and observe that if
\beta=\alpha^{\prime}
then
|v|^{\beta-2}v\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
so that the set

S:=\{v;(|v|^{\alpha^{\prime}-2}v)\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)\}

is non-empty. Consider the definite homogeneous gauge

M(v):=\left\|\nabla(|v|^{\alpha^{\prime}-2}v)\right\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}^{\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}-1}},\quad\textup{ for all }v\in S,

and define the set

\mathscr{M}:=\{v\in S;M(v)\leq 1\}.

An application of the Poincaré–Friedrichs inequality gives that there exists a constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(\Omega)>0
such that

1\geq M(v)\geq c_{P}^{-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}-1}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|v|^{(\alpha^{\prime}-1)p}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/(p(\alpha^{\prime}-1))}=c_{P}^{-\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}-1}}\|v\|_{L^{(\alpha^{\prime}-1)p}(\Omega)}.

It is then possible to show that
\mathscr{M}
is relatively compact in
L^{(\alpha^{\prime}-1)p}(\Omega)
so that an application of Lemma
4.25
with
A_{0}=L^{(\alpha^{\prime}-1)p}(\Omega)
,
A_{1}=W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
,
q_{0}=q_{1}=2
gives

|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\to w_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{(\alpha^{\prime}-1)p}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

where, once again, the monotonicity of the mapping
\xi\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto|\xi|^{\alpha-2}\xi
, the first convergence in the process (
7.1
) and Theorem 12.5-2
(a)
of
[
21
]
imply that:

w_{\ell}=|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}.

(ii)

Preliminary remarks for the recovery of the weak formulation (
4.10
)
.
Let
v\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
and let
\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T])
. For each
0\leq n\leq N-1
, multiply (
4.10
) by
\{v\psi(nk)\}
, getting:

(7.2)

\displaystyle\dfrac{\psi(nk)}{k}\int_{\Omega}\{|u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-|u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\}v\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\psi(nk)\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})|\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla v\,\mathrm{d}x-\psi(nk)\dfrac{\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})}{\ell}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}v\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)v\psi(nk)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Define the mapping
\psi_{k}:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
in a way that
\psi_{k}(t):=\psi(nk)
, for all
nk\leq t<(n+1)k
. Observe that the following estimate holds:

|\psi_{k}(t)-\psi(t)|\leq\omega(k):=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]\\
|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq k\end{subarray}}|\psi(t_{1})-\psi(t_{2})|,\quad\textup{ for all }t\in[0,T].

Since the right-hand side of the estimate above is independent of
t
, and since the continuity of
\psi
implies that
\omega(k)\to 0
as
N\to\infty
, we obtain that
\|\psi_{k}-\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T)}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
and thus, by Hölder’s inequality, in
L^{r}(0,T)
for all
4\leq r\leq\infty
. By virtue of (
6.7
), we are in position to apply Theorem
6.3
and Lemma
6.2
(f)
and deduce, in particular, that
\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\to\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})
in
L^{q}(0,T)
, for any
4\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
, as
N\to\infty
. An application of the generalised Hölder inequality (cf., e.g., Remark 2 on page 93 in
[
11
]
) gives that:

(7.3)

\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\psi_{k}\to\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi,\quad\textup{ in }L^{\upsilon}(0,T),

as
N\to\infty
where
\upsilon
is defined in a way that
\frac{1}{\upsilon}:=\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q}
and, moreover, it results
2\leq\upsilon<6
.

Multiplying (
7.2
) by
k
and summing over
0\leq n\leq N-1
gives:

(7.4)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}D_{k}(|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})v\,\mathrm{d}x\psi_{k}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\int_{\Omega}\nabla\cdot\left(|\nabla(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})|^{p-2}\nabla(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})\right)v\,\mathrm{d}x\psi_{k}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\int_{\Omega}\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}^{-}v\,\mathrm{d}x\psi_{k}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)v\,\mathrm{d}x\right)\psi_{k}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Letting
N\to\infty
in (
7.4
) and exploiting the convergence process (
7.1
), item
(i)
, and (
7.3
) give:

(7.5)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\langle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}\right),v\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\langle g_{\ell}(t),v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi(t)\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}v\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)v\,\mathrm{d}x\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Since by the Sobolev embedding theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 8.4-1 of
[
21
]
) it results
L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, an integration by parts in (
7.5
), and the fact that
u_{\ell}\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha}(\Omega))
(Lemma
4.5
) give:

(7.6)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}v\,\mathrm{d}x\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\psi}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\,\mathrm{d}t+\langle|u_{\ell}(T)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(T),\psi(T)v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell}(0)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(0)\psi(0)v\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\langle g_{\ell}(t),v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi(t)\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}v\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)v\,\mathrm{d}x\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Observe that the first term on the left-hand side of equation (
7.4
) can be rearranged as follows

(7.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell,k}^{N}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{N}v\psi(T-k)\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}|u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0}v\psi(0)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega}|\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)|^{\alpha-2}\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)v\psi^{\prime}(\theta nk+(1-\theta)(1+n)k)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t))\cdot\nabla(\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\psi_{k}(t)v)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega}\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}\left(\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\psi_{k}(t)v\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(\tau)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)v\psi_{k}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

for some
0<\theta<1
(cf., e.g., Theorem 9.10-1
(c)
of
[
21
]
).
Thanks to (
7.1
), item
(i)
, Lemma
6.2
(f)
, and the fact that
\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T])
, we obtain that letting
N\to\infty
in (
7.7
) gives:

(7.8)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}v\,\mathrm{d}x\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\psi}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\langle g_{\ell}(t),v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\left(\chi_{\ell}\psi(T)-|u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0}\psi(0)\right)v\,\mathrm{d}x-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}\left(\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\psi(t)v\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)v\,\mathrm{d}x\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Comparing equations (
7.8
) and (
7.6
) gives:

(7.9)

\displaystyle\langle|u_{\ell}(T)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(T),\psi(T)v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}-\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell}(0)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(0)\psi(0)v\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left(\chi_{\ell}\psi(T)-|u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0}\psi(0)\right)v\,\mathrm{d}x.

Since
\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T])
is arbitrarily chosen, specialising
\psi
in (
7.9
) in a way such that
\psi(0)=0
gives:

(7.10)

\langle|u_{\ell}(T)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(T)-\chi_{\ell},\psi(T)v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}=0.

Since the duality in (
7.10
) is continuous with respect to
v
, since
v
has been chosen arbitrarily in
\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
, and since
\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
is, by definition, dense in
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
, and since
\chi_{\ell}\in L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, we infer that:

(7.11)

|u_{\ell}(T)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(T)=\chi_{\ell}\in L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega).

Specialising
\psi
in (
7.9
) in a way such that
\psi(T)=0
gives:

(7.12)

\int_{\Omega}\left(|u_{\ell}(0)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(0)-|u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0}\right)\psi(0)v\,\mathrm{d}x=0.

Since the integration in (
7.12
) is continuous with respect to
v
, since
v
has been chosen arbitrarily in
\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
, and since
\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
is, by definition, dense in
W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
, we immediately infer that

|u_{\ell}(0)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}(0)=|u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0},

so that the injectivity of the monotone and hemi-continuous operator
\xi\mapsto|\xi|^{\alpha-2}\xi
in turn implies that:

(7.13)

u_{\ell}(0)=u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}.

(iii)

Identification of the weak-star limit
g_{\ell}
via Minty’s trick
. For each
0\leq n\leq N-1
, we multiply (
4.10
) by
ku_{\ell,k}^{n+1}
, sum over
n=0,\dots,N-1
, and apply Lemma
4.7
, thus getting

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\{\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{\alpha^{\prime}}-\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{n}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{n}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{\alpha^{\prime}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}k\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})\left\langle B_{\ell}(u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}),u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}k\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1})\|\{u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}k\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{1}{k}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\tilde{a}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\right)u_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\,\mathrm{d}x,

which in turn implies:

(7.14)

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\||u_{\ell,k}^{N}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell,k}^{N}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{\alpha^{\prime}}+\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\left\langle B_{\ell}(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}),\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\|\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\||u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{\alpha^{\prime}}.

Combining the fact that
\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\to\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})
in
L^{q}(0,T)
, for any
4\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
, as
N\to\infty
(itself consequence of (
6.7
) in the particular case where
4\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
, and Lemma
6.2
(f)
), with the fact that
\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))
independently of
N
and
\ell
(viz. (
7.1
)), the properties of the
\liminf
, and the properties of weak convergence gives:

(7.15)

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\left(\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\|\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{N\to\infty}\bigg(\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\right)\|\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\bigg)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{N\to\infty}\left(\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|\{\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle\geq\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Passing to the
\liminf
as
N\to\infty
in (
7.14
), we obtain that combining the convergence process (
7.1
) with (
7.11
), (
7.13
) and (
7.15
) gives:

(7.16)

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\|u_{\ell}(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}+\liminf_{N\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\left\langle B_{\ell}(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}),\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)u_{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\left\||u_{0}|^{\alpha-2}u_{0}\right\|_{L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)}^{\alpha^{\prime}}-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)u_{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Observe that
u_{\ell}
regarded as an element of
L^{r}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))
with
4\leq r<\infty
can be approximated, in the sense of the strong convergence in
L^{r}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))
, by a function of the form
(\psi(t)v(x))
, where
\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}[0,T]
and
v\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
(cf., e.g., Theorem 8.21 in
[
52
]
).
An application of the triangle inequality thus gives that there exists a sequence
\{\psi_{k,\ell}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T])
and a sequence
\{v_{N,\ell}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
such that

(7.17)

\psi_{k,\ell}v_{N,\ell}\to u_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{r}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

for all
4\leq r<\infty
. In light of (
6.7
) and Lemma
6.2
(f)
it results, in particular, that
\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\to\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})
in
L^{q}(0,T)
as
N\to\infty
, for any
4\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
. Define the real number
s
by
\frac{1}{s}:=\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}
, and observe that
s
varies in the range
2\leq s<6
being
4\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
and
4\leq r<\infty
. An application of (
2.21
), of the convergence
\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\to\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})
in
L^{q}(0,T)
as
N\to\infty
for any
4\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
recalled beforehand, and of the generalised Hölder inequality give:

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\psi_{k,\ell}(t)v_{N,\ell}-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})u_{\ell}(t)\right\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{s}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/s}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\psi_{k,\ell}(t)v_{N,\ell}-u_{\ell}(t)\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{s}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/s}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})-\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\right|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/q}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|u_{\ell}(t)\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}^{r}\right)^{1/r}.

Thanks to (
2.21
), (
7.17
), (
7.3
)and the boundedness of
\{u_{\ell}\}_{\ell>0}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))
established in (
7.1
), the right-hand side of the estimate above tends to zero as
N\to\infty
, showing that

(7.18)

\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\psi_{k,\ell}v_{N,\ell}\to\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})u_{\ell},\quad\textup{ in }L^{s}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))\textup{ as }N\to\infty,

for some
2\leq s<6
.
An application of Lemma
4.19
and (
7.18
) to (
7.5
) gives:

(7.19)

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\|u_{\ell}(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}+\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\langle g_{\ell}(t),u_{\ell}(t)\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)u_{\ell}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\dfrac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\|\{u_{\ell}(t)\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Combining (
7.16
) and (
7.19
) gives:

(7.20)

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\left\langle B_{\ell}(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}),\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\langle g_{\ell}(t),u_{\ell}(t)\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t.

We now exploit the method of Minty, known as Minty’s trick
[
58
]
. Let
w\in L^{p}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))
. An application of (
2.21
), of the (strict) monotonicity of the operator
B_{\ell}
defined in (
5.36
), and (
7.20
) give:

(7.21)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\langle g_{\ell}-B_{\ell}(w),u_{\ell}-w\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{1}\liminf_{N\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\langle B_{\ell}(\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k})-B_{\ell}(w),\Pi_{k}\bm{u}_{\ell,k}-w\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

Let
\lambda>0
and specialise
w=u_{\ell}-\lambda v
in (
7.21
), where
v
is arbitrarily chosen in
L^{p}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))
. We obtain that:

(7.22)

\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\langle g_{\ell}-B_{\ell}(u_{\ell}-\lambda v),\lambda v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

Dividing (
7.22
) by
\lambda>0
, letting
\lambda\to 0^{+}
and exploiting the hemi-continuity of
B_{\ell}
gives:

\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\langle g_{\ell}-B_{\ell}(u_{\ell}),v\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\geq 0.

Specialising
v=\psi(t)\tilde{v}
, with
\psi\in\mathcal{D}(0,T)
and
\tilde{v}\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
, we obtain that:

\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})\langle g_{\ell}-B_{\ell}(u_{\ell}),\tilde{v}\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}=0,\quad\textup{ for a.a. }t\in(0,T).

Since
\mu_{1}\leq\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})<\mu_{2}
independently of
\ell
by (
2.21
) and since
\tilde{v}
is chosen arbitrarily in
\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
, the latter implies:

g_{\ell}=B_{\ell}(u_{\ell})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)).

(iv)

Preliminary remarks for the recovery of the weak formulation (
4.9
)
. Let
\Xi\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega\times(0,L))
and let
\psi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T])
. For each
0\leq n\leq N-1
, multiply (
4.11
) by
\{\psi(nk)\Xi\}
, getting:

(7.23)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\dfrac{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n}}{k}\psi(nk)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x+\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}(\psi(nk)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\cdot\nabla(\psi(nk)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\}^{-}(\psi(nk)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\psi(nk)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}h_{\ell,k}^{n}(\psi(nk)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\psi(nk)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}x.

Define the mapping
\psi_{k}:[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
in a way that
\psi_{k}(t):=\psi(nk)
, for all
nk\leq t<(n+1)k
.
Multiplying (
7.23
) by
k
and summing over
n=0,\dots,N-1
gives:

(7.24)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}D_{k}(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\psi_{k}(t)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\nabla(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k})\cdot\nabla(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}\}^{-}(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\Lambda_{k}\bm{h}_{\ell,k}(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

An application of Theorem
6.3
, the properties of finite difference quotients (viz., e.g., Section 5.8.2 in
[
30
]
), and the affinity and continuity of the
-\Delta_{b}:L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\to L^{\infty}(0,T;V^{\prime})
(both inherited by the affinity and continuity of the original
elliptic
operator
-\Delta_{b}:V\to V^{\prime}
) allow us to pass to the limit as
N\to\infty
in (
7.24
), so as to obtain

(7.25)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\langle-\Delta_{b}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}),\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\}^{-}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}h_{\ell}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

on the one hand.
On the other hand, observe that the first term in (
7.23
) can be written as:

\displaystyle\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{N}\psi(T-k)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\dfrac{\rho c}{k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\psi(0)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\rho c\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{n+1}\left[\dfrac{\psi((n+1)k)-\psi(nk)}{k}\right]\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x.

Multiplying (
7.23
) by
k
, summing over
n=0,\dots,N-1
, and applying the Taylor–MacLaurin formula (cf., e.g., Theorem 9.10-1
(c)
in
[
21
]
) to the latter leads to:

(7.26)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell,k}^{N}\psi(T-k)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\psi(0)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\rho c\sum_{n=0}^{N-2}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t)\psi^{\prime}(\theta nk+(1-\theta)(1+n)k)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\rho c\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t))(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\nabla(\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t))\cdot\nabla(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t)\}^{-}(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\Pi_{k}\tilde{\bm{\Theta}}_{\ell,k}(t)-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\psi_{k}(t)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\Lambda_{k}\bm{h}_{\ell,k}(t)(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t-\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{nk}^{(n+1)k}\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\psi_{k}(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

An application of Theorem
6.3
and (
7.1
) allow us to pass to the limit as
N\to\infty
in (
7.26
), so as to obtain:

(7.27)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\tau}_{\ell}\psi(T)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\psi(0)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\Xi\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\psi}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\langle-\Delta_{b}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(t)),\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\}^{-}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}h_{\ell}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Taking an integration by parts in time of (
7.25
) leads to:

(7.28)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(T)\psi(T)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x-\rho c\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(0)\psi(0)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\left(\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\psi}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\Xi\right)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\bm{U}(0),v_{z}(0))\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\langle-\Delta_{b}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell},\Xi\rangle_{V^{\prime},V}\psi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\}^{-}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}h_{\ell}(\psi(t)\Xi)\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Therefore, subtracting (
7.28
) from (
7.27
) gives:

(7.29)

\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\tilde{\tau}_{\ell}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(T))\psi(T)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\Omega\times(0,L)}(\tilde{\Theta}_{0}-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(0))\psi(0)\Xi\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x.

Specialising
\psi
in (
7.29
) first in a way that
\psi(T)=0
and, second, in a way that
\psi(0)=0
leads to, respectively:

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(0)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Theta}_{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}(T)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\tau}_{\ell}.

(v)

Completion of the proof
. Putting together steps
(i)
–
(iv)
shows that Problem

P
ℓ

admits at least one solution
(u_{\ell},\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell})
, as it was to be proved.
∎

8.
Recovery of the limit model (
3.25
)–(
3.31
)

The next step, which constitutes the final step of the existence result we want to establish, is the passage to the limit as the penalty parameter approaches zero, as well as the recovery of a sound
concept of solution
for the model governing the evolution of time-dependent thermally-coupled shallow ice sheets. We mention that the strategy proposed here solves a mistake in the proof proposed in
[
63
]
for the recovery for the limit model. While the mistake in
[
63
]
affects the identification of the concept of solution, the idea itself is valid and the correct result can still be reached by arguing in a different fashion.

The estimates (
5.1
)–(
5.11
), the estimates (
5.12
), (
5.16
), (
5.17
) and Theorem
7.1
imply that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
\ell
such that:

(8.1)

\displaystyle\|u_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\left\||u_{\ell}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\left\|\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|u_{\ell}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell})\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\left\||u_{\ell}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\|u_{\ell}(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\|\{u_{\ell}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\ell},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;V)}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\left\|\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\right\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))}

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}\}^{-}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\ell},

\displaystyle\|\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\ell}.

By the Banach–Alaoglu–Bourbaki theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 3.6 of
[
11
]
) we infer that, up to passing to subsequences
\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, with
\ell_{n}\to 0^{+}
as
n\to\infty
, it results:

(8.2)

\displaystyle u_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}u,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}v,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell_{n}}\right)\rightharpoonup\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}v}{\,\mathrm{d}t},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}w,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle u_{\ell_{n}}(T)\rightharpoonup\chi,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\alpha}(\Omega)\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{-}\to 0,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\tilde{\Theta},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\rightharpoonup\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}}{\,\mathrm{d}t},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}\}^{-}\to 0,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}\to 0,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

The first convergence in (
8.2
) implies that:

u_{\ell_{n}}\rightharpoonup u,\quad\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\simeq L^{2}((0,T)\times\Omega)\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

The continuity and the monotonicity of the negative part established in Lemma
4.1
(a)
allow us to apply Theorem 12.5-2
(a)
of
[
21
]
, getting

(8.3)

\{u\}^{-}=0\quad\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

which means that
u(t)\geq 0
a.e. in
\Omega
, for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
.

Using the same compactness argument as in Lemma
6.1
, an application of Lemma
4.20
(b)
with
A_{0}:=L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega)
,
A_{1}:=L^{2}(\Omega)
,
q_{0}=\infty
and
q_{1}=2
gives that:

(8.4)

\left\{|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell_{n}}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\textup{ strongly converges in }\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\frac{2p}{\alpha}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

The seventh and eighth convergences in (
8.2
) put us in position to apply the classical Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness theorem, so as to infer that, up to passing to an ulterior subsequence

(8.5)

\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}\to\tilde{\Theta},\quad\textup{ in }\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L))),

as
n\to\infty
, for all
2\leq q<6
.
Since, by Lemma
6.2
(c)
, the operator
\mathcal{A}
is continuous from
L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))
to itself in the particular case where
\frac{10}{3}\leq q\leq\frac{28}{5}
, we obtain that an application of (
8.5
) gives:

(8.6)

\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}})\to\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta}),\quad\textup{ in }L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

In light of (
8.4
) and (
8.6
), we can systematically apply, in this order, Lemma
4.10
, Lemma
4.11
, (
4.4
) and Theorem
6.3
(applied to
\ell
instead of
N
) to obtain that:

(8.7)

h_{\ell}\to h:=\mathcal{A}(\tilde{\Theta})\left(\left\{u^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p},\quad\textup{ in }L^{\sigma}(0,T;L^{\sigma}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

where
\sigma
is the real number defined in Theorem
6.3
.

The same monotonicity argument as in Theorem
7.1
and the third convergence in (
8.2
) in turn imply that the limit
v
of the sequence in (
8.4
) takes the following form:

v=|u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u\in H^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)).

For each
v\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v(|u_{\ell_{n}}|-u)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v(\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{+}+\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{-}-u)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v\left((\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{+}-\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{-})+2\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{-}-u\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v(u_{\ell_{n}}-u)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t+2\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}^{-}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\to 0,

as
n\to\infty
since the first term converges to zero by the first convergence in (
8.2
) and the second term converges to zero thanks to (
8.3
). In conclusion, we have established the following convergence:

(8.8)

|u_{\ell_{n}}|\rightharpoonup u,\quad\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

In light of (
8.4
) and (
8.8
), we are in position to apply Lemma
4.6
so as to obtain

\left\{|u_{\ell_{n}}|\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\textup{ strongly converges in }\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

and, so, it also strongly converges in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{\alpha}(\Omega))
. Combining this with the convergences (
8.3
) and (
8.8
) gives that:

(8.9)

|u_{\ell_{n}}|\to u,\quad\textup{ in }\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

Define the linear and continuous operator
L_{0}:\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha}(\Omega))\to L^{\alpha}(\Omega)
by:

L_{0}(v):=v(0),\quad\textup{ for all }v\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{\alpha}(\Omega)).

By (
8.9
) and the continuity of
L_{0}
, we have that
|u_{\ell_{n}}(0)|\to u(0)
in
L^{\alpha}(\Omega)
as
n\to\infty
. However, since
u_{\ell_{n}}(0)=u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we immediately deduce that
u(0)=u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}
and so that the weak-star limit
u
in (
8.2
) satisfies the expected initial condition.

An application of the first and fourth convergences in (
8.2
) give
u_{\ell_{n}}\rightharpoonup u
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{\alpha}(\Omega))
as
n\to\infty
and
(|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}})\rightharpoonup w
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))
as
n\to\infty
, respectively. Observing that an application of (
8.9
) gives
(|u|^{\alpha-2}u)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))
as well as

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}}\right)u_{\ell_{n}}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\to\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u|^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left(|u|^{\alpha-2}u\right)u\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

as
n\to\infty
, we are in position to apply Theorem 12.5-2
(a)
in
[
21
]
and conclude that:

(8.10)

w=|u|^{\alpha-2}u,\quad\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega)).

Thanks to Lemma
4.5
, Lemma
4.6
and Lemma
4.19
, it results:

(8.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\langle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u_{\ell_{n}}(t)|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}}(t)\right),u_{\ell_{n}}(t)\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t=\dfrac{\|u_{\ell_{n}}(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}-\dfrac{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\to\dfrac{\|u(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}-\dfrac{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}},\quad\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

Define the operator
B:L^{1}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\to L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
as follows:

\displaystyle\langle B(u),v\rangle_{L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)),L^{1}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))}:=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}(t))|\nabla(u(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(u(t))\cdot\nabla(v(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

for all
u,v\in L^{1}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))
.
For any
v\in L^{1}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))
, an application of (
2.21
) and Hölder’s inequality gives:

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\cdot\nabla(v(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\partial_{i}(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\partial_{i}(v(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{2}\Bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Omega}\left||\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\partial_{i}(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\right|^{p/(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{(p-1)/p}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{i}(v(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/p}\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{2}\Bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p(p-2)/(p-1)}|\partial_{i}(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p/(p-1)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{(p-1)/p}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{i}(v(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1/p}\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mu_{2}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}^{p-1}\|\nabla(v(t))\|_{\bm{L}^{p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mu_{2}\left(\sup_{t\in(0,T)}\|u_{\ell_{n}}(t)\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\right)^{p-1}\|v\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))}.

This estimate shows that the sequence
\{B(u_{\ell_{n}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
independently of
n
. Since
\mu
is bounded below by
\mu_{1}>0
(viz. (
2.21
)), we obtain that the sequence
\{-\nabla\cdot(|\nabla u_{\ell_{n}}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell_{n}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
independently of
n
. A subsequent application of the Banach–Alaoglu–Bourbaki theorem (cf., e.g., Theorem 3.6 of
[
11
]
) ensures the existence of an element
G\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
such that:

(8.12)

-\nabla\cdot\left(|\nabla u_{\ell_{n}}|^{p-2}\nabla u_{\ell_{n}}\right)\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}G,\quad\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

The continuity of
\mu
asserted in Lemma
6.2
(e)
, the boundedness of the sequence
\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega))
stated in the first line of (
8.2
), and the sequential weak lower semicontinuity of the convex functional
w\in L^{p}(0,T;W^{1,p}(\Omega))\mapsto\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(w(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t
give:

(8.13)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\left(-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}})|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\right)=-\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})|\nabla(u(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Thanks to Lemma
6.2
(e)
, the first bound in (
8.1
), Hölder’s inequality, and (
8.12
) we obtain that, up to passing to a suitable subsequence, the following convergence holds as
n\to\infty
:

(8.14)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\cdot\nabla(v(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\left(\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}})-\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\right)\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\cdot\nabla(v(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\cdot\nabla\left(\mu(\tilde{\Theta})v(t)\right)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\to\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\langle G(t),v(t)\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Thanks to Lemma
4.18
, we infer that the seventh convergence in (
8.2
) implies:

(8.15)

\widetilde{\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}\rightharpoonup\widetilde{\nabla\tilde{\Theta}},\quad\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;\bm{L}^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

Let
v\in W^{1,2,2}(0,T;W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega);L^{\alpha}(\Omega))
be such that
v(t)\geq 0
in
\overline{\Omega}
(since
p>2
), multiply equation (
4.8
) by
(v(t)-u_{\ell_{n}}(t))
in the sense of the duality between
W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
and
W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
. A subsequent integration in
(0,T)
gives:

(8.16)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left\langle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}}),v(t)-u_{\ell_{n}}(t)\right\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mu(\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}})|\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))|^{p-2}\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\cdot(\nabla(v(t))-\nabla(u_{\ell_{n}}(t)))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)(v(t)-u_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

where the inequality holds thanks to the monotonicity of
(-\{\cdot\}^{-})
established in Lemma
4.1
(a)
and (
2.21
).

Passing to the
\limsup
as
n\to\infty
in (
8.16
), an application of (
8.10
) (note that
(\alpha^{\prime}-1)p>\alpha^{\prime}
), (
8.11
) and (
8.13
) and (
8.14
) give that the weak-star limit
u
satisfies:

(8.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u(T)|^{\alpha-2}u(T)v(T)-u_{0}^{\alpha-1}v(0)\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u(t)|^{\alpha-2}u(t)\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}v}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\|u(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\dfrac{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\langle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{G}{{{}}G}(t),v(t)\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)(v(t)-u(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

Choose
\Xi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;V)
such that
0\leq\Xi(t)\leq\Theta_{\textup{m}}
for a.a.
t\in(0,T)
and multiply equation (
4.9
) by
(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))
in the sense of duality between
V^{\prime}
and
V
. A subsequent integration in
(0,T)
gives:

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(t)+U_{1}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}{\partial x_{1}}(t)+U_{2}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}{\partial x_{2}}(t)+v_{z}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}{\partial z}(t)\right)(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t)\cdot\nabla(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t-\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t)\}^{-}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\ell}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\{\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t)-\Theta_{\textup{m}}\}^{+}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}A(\cdot,\cdot,\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\left(\left\{|u_{\ell_{n}}(t)|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

An application of the seventh and eight convergences in (
8.2
), (
8.5
), (
8.7
), (
8.15
), the monotonicity of
(-\{\cdot\}^{-})
and
\{\cdot\}^{+}
established in Lemma
4.1
(a)
and Remark
4.2
, and the fact that

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}(t)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t,

give that the limit
\tilde{\Theta}
satisfies:

(8.18)

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(t)+U_{1}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}}{\partial x_{1}}(t)+U_{2}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}}{\partial x_{2}}(t)+v_{z}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}}{\partial z}(t)\right)(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\tilde{\Theta}(t)\cdot\nabla(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq 2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}A(\cdot,\cdot,\tilde{\Theta}(t))\left(\left\{u(t)^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t.

In the end, we are in position to write down the model governing the evolution of the thickness of a shallow ice sheet, and to assert that this model admits at least one solution.
This is the main result of this article, the proof of which has been given above.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let
T>0
, let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a Lipschitz domain, let
2.8\leq p\leq 5
and let
\alpha
be defined in (
4.1
).
Assume that (
H
1
)–(
H
3
) hold.
Then, the family
\{u_{\ell}\}_{\ell>0}
of solutions of Problem

P
ℓ

generated by Theorem
7.1
satisfies the following convergences up to passing to a subsequence
\{u_{\ell_{n}}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, where
\{\ell_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is such that
\ell_{n}\to 0^{+}
as
n\to\infty
:

\displaystyle u_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}u,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}|u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u_{\ell_{n}}\right)\rightharpoonup\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u\right),

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}|u|^{\alpha-2}u,

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\alpha^{\prime}}(\Omega))\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}\overset{\ast}{\rightharpoonup}\tilde{\Theta},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{\infty}(0,T;V)\textup{ as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell_{n}}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\rightharpoonup\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}}{\,\mathrm{d}t},

\displaystyle\textup{ in }L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L)))\textup{ as }n\to\infty.

Moreover, the limit
(u,\tilde{\Theta})
is a solution to the following variational problem:

Problem
\mathcal{P}
.

Find
u\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{Ksurf2}{{{}}\mathcal{K}_{\textup{surf}}}:=\{v\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega));v(t)\in K_{\textup{surf}}\textup{ a.e. in }(0,T)\}
and
\tilde{\Theta}\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{mathsymbols}{Ktemp2}{{{}}\mathcal{K}_{\textup{temp}}}:=\{\Xi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;V);0\leq\Xi(t)\leq\Theta_{\textup{m}}\textup{ a.e. in }(0,T)\}
such that

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\left(|u|^{\frac{\alpha-2}{2}}u\right)

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}

\displaystyle\in L^{\infty}(0,T;V),

\displaystyle\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega\times(0,L))),

and find
G\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
satisfying the variational inequalities (
8.17
) and (
8.18
), namely,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u(T)|^{\alpha-2}u(T)v(T)-u_{0}^{\alpha-1}v(0)\,\mathrm{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}|u(t)|^{\alpha-2}u(t)\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}v}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(t)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\dfrac{\|u(T)\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\dfrac{\|u_{0}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\Omega)}^{\alpha}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\langle G(t),v(t)\rangle_{W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega),W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\mu(\tilde{\Theta})\int_{\Omega}|\nabla(u(t))|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t\geq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}(t)(v(t)-u(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

and

\displaystyle\rho c\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\left(\dfrac{\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(t)+U_{1}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}}{\partial x_{1}}(t)+U_{2}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}}{\partial x_{2}}(t)+v_{z}(0)\dfrac{\partial\tilde{\Theta}}{\partial z}(t)\right)(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}\nabla\tilde{\Theta}(t)\cdot\nabla(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq 2(\rho gC_{0})^{p}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{\Omega}A(\cdot,\cdot,\tilde{\Theta}(t))\left(\left\{u(t)^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}-z\right\}^{+}\right)^{p}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}(\Xi(t)-\tilde{\Theta}(t))\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}t,

for all
v\in W^{1,2,2}(0,T;W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega);L^{\alpha}(\Omega))
such that
v(t)\geq 0
in
\overline{\Omega}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, and for all
\Xi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;V)
such that
0\leq\Xi(t)\leq\Theta_{\textup{m}}
for a.a.
t\in(0,T)
.

Moreover, the functions
u
and
\tilde{\Theta}
satisfy the following initial conditions

\displaystyle u(0)

\displaystyle=u_{0},

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}(0)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Theta}_{0},

for some prescribed
u_{0}\in K_{\textup{surf}}
and
\tilde{\Theta}_{0}\in K_{\textup{temp}}
.

∎

It is worth observing that a potential identification of the weak-star limit
G
with the
expected
term
-\nabla\cdot(|\nabla u|^{p-2}\nabla u)
hinges, for instance, on the higher regularity for the term
\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|u|^{\alpha-2}u)
. In general, we cannot say that the term
\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|u|^{\alpha-2}u)
is the weak-star limit of the sequence
\{\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}(|u_{\ell_{n}}|^{\alpha-2}u_{\ell_{n}})\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega))
as
n\to\infty
, as this sequence is in general not bounded independently of
n
(viz. (
5.34
); where
n
refer to the index of the subsequence
\{\ell_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
).

Conclusions

In this article we proposed a new model governing the simultaneous evolution of the surface elevation in a shallow ice sheet lying on a flat bedrock and its internal temperature.
Since the ice internal temperature is defined over a
moving domain
, whose shape is determined by the surface elevation of the shallow ice sheet under consideration, and since both the ice surface elevation and the ice internal temperature are subjected to physical constraints, the model takes the form of a coupled system of evolutionary variational inequalities. In order to remove the presence of a boundary term, in the
thermal
variational inequalities, depending on the surface elevation of the shallow ice sheet under consideration, we apply a
corrector
that cuts the ice internal temperature off near the ice surface. This gives rise to a model resembling to the diffuse-interface ones that have been widely studied in the literature for the Cahn–Hilliard equation. The dependence of the approximate model on the parameter
r_{0}
still remains, as the initial condition is chosen in a way that it complies with the construction performed in Section
3
.

The intrinsic degeneracy for the model governing the evolution of the surface elevation in the shallow ice sheet under consideration inserts an additional layer of difficulty, that we overcome by resorting to a change of variables originally suggested by P.-A. Raviart
[
65
]
.

In order to establish the existence of solutions for the problem under consideration, we first define a
penalised
version for the formal model we derived, and we establish the existence of solutions for this penalised model by finite differences in time. Finally, we pass to the limit in the penalty parameter and we recover a model that agrees with the one we had recovered formally.

List of Mathematical Symbols

$\mathcal{C}^{0}(\overline{I};X)$

space of $X$-valued functions that are continuous on $\overline{I}$

$\mathcal{D}(\omega)$

space of smooth functions with compact support in the open set $\omega$

$\ell$

positive penalty parameter meant to approach zero

$L^{p}(I;X)$

space of $X$-valued functions that are $L^{p}$ integrable in the interval $I$

$N$

number of equally-spaced intervals the interval $[0,T]$ is divided into

$\{\cdot\}^{+}$

positive part operator defined pointwise by $\{f\}^{+}=\max\{f,0\}$

$u_{\ell}$

Solution for~\eqref{penalty-u} in Problem~\ref{Pkappa}

$W^{m,p}(I;X)$

space of $X$-valued functions $v:I\toX$ such that $v,v^{\prime},\dots,v^{(m)}\inL^{p}(I;X)$

$W^{1,p,q}(I;X)$

space of $X$-valued functions $v:I\toX$ such that $v\inL^{p}(I;X)$ and $v^{\prime}\inL^{q}(I;X)$

$X^{\prime}$

dual space of the normed vector space $X$

$\bm{\nu}_{\partial\Omega}$

outer unit normal vector along $\partial\Omega$

$\Omega$

domain in $\mathbb{R}^{2}$

$\tilde{\Theta}_{\ell}$

Solution for~\eqref{penalty-thetatilde} in Problem~\ref{Pkappa}

List of Physics Symbols

$a_{b}$

basal melting rate

$a_{s}$

surface mass balance

$g$

gravitational acceleration

$L$

positive constant associated with the maximum ice thickness

$q_{\textup{geo}}^{\perp}$

positive geothermal heat flux from bedrock to ice bulk

$r_{0}$

diffuse interface parameter

$\bm{\nu}_{b}$

unit vector normal to the bedrock which points towards the portion of three-dimensional space that does not contain the bedrock

$\bm{\nu}_{\Gamma_{f,t}}$

outer unit normal vector along the free boundary $\Gamma_{f,t}$

$\bm{\nu}_{\text{ice}}$

outer unit normal vector applied along the surface $\{(x,h(t,x));x\in\overline{\Omega_{t}^{+}}\}$ and pointing towards to portion of space complementary to the one occupied by ice at time $t\in[0,T]$

$\Theta_{0}$

initial shallow ice sheet internal temperature

$\Theta_{\textup{int}}$

internal auxiliary temperature
